5a

Pricing power

3 answers
C-1762C-4538P-0455
C-17To what extent is the company raising prices in real terms (above cost inflation), and are the rises real, merely nominal, or so far only latent or plausible and increasingly noted by management?A621st of 11full answer under 2b Internal corporate change forces
C-45To what extent does the company have, and use, demonstrable pricing power with minimal erosion of its relative surplus customer utility, shown by evidence of application rather than conjecture?F3810th of 11full answer under 4a Business quality
P-04

After assessing the pricing power in greater detail, how is price likely to contribute to revenue and margin over the next 18 months to four years?

A551st of 9

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: Price is not a growth driver for Redcare. It contributes one discrete, state-granted step in 2027 (the fixed fee rising to €9.50, net of the higher rebate), worth about €8-9m of EBITDA year on year (about 20bp), of which roughly a third is likely to be handed back to patients through co-payment cover as competition responds. Beyond 2027 the net contribution of price to the margin is slightly negative (Swiss price cuts, bonus recycling, OTC price transparency) unless the annual fee adjustment due from 2028 delivers indexation, which is real upside not in my base case. Over FY26-FY30, price adds about 0.3% to FY27 group revenue growth and roughly nothing thereafter, and adds about 10bp net to the FY30 margin.

Price by business line:

Business line (FY26 share of revenue) Price mechanism Contribution to revenue Contribution to margin
German Rx (22%) Fixed by the state: fee €8.35 to €9.00 (July 2026) to €9.50 (January 2027); rebate to insurers €1.77 to €2.07 (January 2027); 3% of the drug price +1.5-2% on German Rx revenue in FY27 (about €13m), then only drug-price pass-through FY27 +€8-9m year on year (the H2 2026 step, €3-4m, is already in FY26); nothing after unless indexed
Rx bonuses and co-payment cover (a negative price) Legal for EU mail-order pharmacies (Federal Court of Justice, 17 July 2025); form restricted (Karlsruhe, 9 December 2025, €25 vouchers; ECJ, February 2025, OTC-redeemable vouchers) Booked against revenue or gross margin My assumption: -€6m in FY27, -€10m in FY28, -€12m a year thereafter (incremental to the 2026 level)
Swiss Rx through MediService (19%) Federal Office of Public Health triennial price cuts; generic substitution; uniform margin system since July 2024 About -1 to -2% a year on price, inside 8-10% growth About -5bp a year at group level (MediService margin is very thin)
DACH non-Rx and International (60%) Redcare is a deliberate price-giver (up to 10% below rivals on 30-50% of the range, often via vouchers); AI pricing optimises mix Flat price; mix slightly positive About +5bp a year of gross margin from AI pricing and own brands
Retail media and marketplace (under 5%) The one line where Redcare sets prices; more than 150 suppliers Small About +10bp a year (counted in P-01 and P-03 as monetisation)

The fee step, checked per pack. Deutsche Bank (3 June 2026) puts the 2027 benefit against the pre-July-2026 regime at €13.3m (17.6m packs at €50) or €9.5m (12.5m packs at €70), after the rebate and the €0.19 signature cost. Since about €3-4m arrived in H2 2026, the year-on-year gain in FY27 is about €6-9m, not the €9.5-13.3m often quoted; my figure is €8.5m on about 16m packs (my FY27 German Rx revenue of €960m at about €60 a pack). From 2028 the remuneration is to be reviewed annually with reference to inflation and costs (Deutsche Bank, 1 June 2026). An indexation of 2-3% a year (about €0.20-0.30 a pack) would add €4-6m a year, about 10bp; I leave it out because the Health Finance Commission's €42.3bn savings agenda and rebate proposals point the other way.

The co-payment contest is the decisive pricing variable for 2027-28. Patient co-payments rise 50% in 2027 (minimum €5 to €7.50, maximum €10 to €15). The co-payment does not change the pharmacy's price; it changes who pays. It matters because only EU mail-order pharmacies may waive it, so the value of their bonus to a chronic patient rises by €2.50-5 a pack. Three facts frame the likely outcome: both leaders are in margin mode (DocMorris declined at its August 2026 roadshow to commit to covering the full increase; Redcare's guidance stresses cash generation); the Karlsruhe and ECJ rulings constrain the form; and Redcare has the largest fee gain to recycle (€9.5-13.3m against DocMorris' €3.7-5.2m, Deutsche Bank). My base case is partial, targeted cover for high-value chronic cohorts, costing Redcare about a third of its 2027 fee gain in FY27 and rising slightly in FY28. A full-cover war (both cover the whole increase for all patients) would cost Redcare roughly €2.50-5 on perhaps half of its 16m packs, €20-40m a year, and erase the fee step and Sevenum together; I put that at about 15% probability over 18 months, triggered most plausibly by DocMorris using its refinanced balance sheet or by a growth-minded new Redcare management.

Why the gross margin percentage will keep falling, and why it does not matter much. Mix explains about 50bp of the 170bp fall in Q2 2026 and will take a further 20-30bp a year off the gross margin percentage as German Rx rises from 22% to 30% of revenue. Gross profit in euros still grows about 11-12% a year. At EBITDA level the mix effect is only about -3 to -5bp a year (P-01), because Rx carries half the fulfilment cost per euro of revenue.

Peer reference. Apotea held a 27.5% gross margin (Q2 2026) as Rx rose, through cross-selling and own brands; that is the template for non-Rx, and Redcare's mixed-order rate rising from 34.9% to 40.8% shows the same mechanism working. CVS Group's Animed Direct earns about 3% EBITDA under price-comparison pressure and fell 8.2% in FY25 on down-trading: the cautionary case for Redcare's non-Rx half. Galenica offsets Swiss price cuts by adding paid pharmacy services, an option Redcare does not have.

Implication for the model. Model German Rx gross profit per pack explicitly from the fee schedule, deduct co-payment cover as a separate negative price, hold non-Rx price flat, and let the gross margin percentage fall about 20-30bp a year without treating that as margin erosion.

5b

Operating leverage

2 answers
C-7562P-0162
C-75

How material is the fixed-cost operating leverage: how much does a given change in revenue move operating profit, and how wide a range of outcomes does that create?

B+622nd of 11

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: Very material. On today's 3% EBITDA margin, the incremental margin is about 8-12%, so every revenue deviation is amplified three to four times at EBITDA. Below EBITDA the amplification is larger again, because D&A and interest are largely fixed.

Evidence of incremental margin:

  • Q2 2026: revenue +€144m year on year; adjusted EBITDA +€11.6m. That is 8.1% incremental, even while marketing absorbed growth.
  • H1 2026: revenue +€276m; EBITDA +€17m (6.2%).
  • Consensus FY27: +€438m revenue for +€39m EBITDA (8.9%).
  • Consensus FY28: +€419m for +€44m (10.5%).

Cost structure (my estimate, because the split is not disclosed):

  • Variable: cost of goods (about 78-80% of revenue); shipping and packaging (about €3 per parcel); payment fees; performance marketing and bonuses.
  • Semi-fixed: warehouse labour. This becomes more fixed once Sevenum is automated, cutting labour cost per order by 70%.
  • Fixed: IT and technology, brand marketing, G&A, depreciation, building costs.
  • The contribution margin on an incremental German Rx pack is high: a fixed fee of about €7.43 net per pack from 2027, plus a small percentage, less about €2-3 of pick, pack and ship per pack. The contribution on incremental OTC is lower (30% gross margin minus about €4-5 per order of fulfilment on a smaller basket).

Sensitivity (illustrative). A 5% revenue shortfall against FY27 consensus (about €195m) at a 9-12% contribution would cost:

  • about €18-23m of EBITDA (13-17% of €138m);
  • about €0.65-0.85 of EPS (50-65% of €1.32).

The symmetrical upside applies. Management can flex marketing to defend margin, which turns a revenue miss into a growth miss rather than a margin miss, and vice versa.

Precedents:

  • Negative leverage in 2021: Sevenum migration, an Rx bonus ban, Q2 growth of 7.3% and losses.
  • Negative leverage in Q4 2025: promotions took the margin to 1.6%.
  • Positive leverage in Q2 2026.

Range of outcomes.

  • EBITDA in FY27: about €115-160m (3.0-4.0%) across plausible revenue and marketing paths.
  • EPS: about €0.5 to €2.2.

That is a wide cone. Its centre is moving up with the evidence of 2026.

P-01

After assessing the fixed-cost operating leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, against the last four to six years?

A621st of 9

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: Operating leverage is real and will lift the adjusted EBITDA margin by about 40-65bp a year, from 2.9% in FY26 to about 4.85% in FY30. It is roughly half as powerful as the 2026 figures suggest, because most of the 2026 step came from re-routing acquisition spending rather than from fixed costs being spread over more volume. The leverage that remains is steady and forecastable (cohort layering, Sevenum, overheads), which makes the margin path more predictable than at any point in the last six years, but slower than the company's "5% by FY28".

Why the 2026 evidence overstates the lever (the decomposition the Consumer needs). Q2 2026 against Q2 2025, on the company's basis:

Component Q2 2025 Q2 2026 Effect on the EBITDA margin
Gross margin 23.8% 22.1% -170bp
of which Rx mix (German Rx from 16% to 21% of revenue) about -50bp
of which bonuses, retail media timing and other about -120bp
Marketing 7.2% 5.1% +210bp
All other operating costs (fulfilment, IT, administration) 14.1% 13.5% +60bp
Adjusted EBITDA margin 2.5% 3.5% +100bp

The same picture holds for H1: selling and distribution costs fell 250bp (18.9% to 16.4%), of which 210bp was marketing, so all other selling and distribution costs fell only about 40bp. The conclusion: the combined cost of acquiring and rewarding patients fell by roughly 90bp of revenue, and genuine fixed-cost leverage was about 40-60bp at 20% growth. Neither can be repeated at that scale: marketing is now at management's stated floor of about 5%, and growth slows to 11-13%.

The cost structure, as far as it can be established (my estimate; the split is not disclosed).

  • Variable with revenue or orders (about 92-93% of revenue): cost of goods (about 78% of revenue); shipping (about €3 a parcel, plus €0.19 for the signature rule on Rx); packaging; payment fees; performance marketing; Rx bonuses and co-payment cover (booked against gross margin); warehouse labour until Sevenum is automated.
  • Fixed or step-fixed (about 3.5-4% of revenue, about €130m): IT and technology, administration (2.6% of revenue in H1 2026), brand marketing and management. These still rise with wages at about 4-5% a year, so they create leverage only on the gap between revenue growth and cost inflation.
  • The arithmetic that follows: at 13% revenue growth, fixed-cost absorption adds about 25-35bp a year; at 10% growth, about 20-25bp.

The contribution of an extra euro of revenue. An incremental German Rx pack (about €50-60 of revenue excluding VAT) earns about €8.8 of gross profit in 2027 (fixed fee €9.50 plus 3% of the purchase price, less the €2.07 rebate), less about €2.3 of fulfilment (two packs per parcel on average), less payment costs and an average bonus of about €1, leaving about €5-6, or about 9-10% of revenue. Incremental non-Rx earns about 30% gross margin less about 14% fulfilment, payment fees and about 5% performance marketing, also about 9-10%. So the shift to Rx dilutes the gross margin percentage but is almost neutral at EBITDA level, because Rx carries lower fulfilment cost per euro of revenue (DocMorris: 7% of sales for Rx against 14% for OTC). The falling gross margin percentage is not a margin problem and the Consumer should not model it as one.

How the line items move, against the last six years.

Line FY20-FY25 (history) FY26-FY30 (my forecast) What changes
Revenue growth 9.5% to 49.4%; about 20% organic 18.1% falling to 9.7% Half the growth rate; far steadier mix
Gross margin (company basis) 22.6-23.0% (FY24-25), 28% before MediService about 21.7% falling to about 21.0% German Rx rises from 17% to 30% of revenue
Marketing above 7% (FY24-25) 5.3% (FY26) to about 4.6% (FY30) Cohort layering, about 10-20bp a year after FY27
All other operating costs about 14% 13.5% to about 12.0% Fixed-cost absorption and Sevenum
Adjusted EBITDA margin -0.3% to 3.0%, average about 1.5%, volatile 2.9%, 3.55%, 4.0%, 4.45%, 4.85% Steady rise of 40-65bp a year
D&A excluding PPA about €55-60m €57m rising to €80m Sevenum adds about €9m from 2027
EBIT margin excluding PPA about -1% to 0% 1.3% rising to 3.3% Operating leverage amplified about 1.6x at EBIT

The bridge from FY26 (2.9%) to FY30 (4.85%), in basis points of revenue: fixed-cost absorption +100; marketing from 5.3% to about 4.6% +65; Sevenum net savings +45; retail media, marketplace and own brands +40; the 2027 fee step net of the rebate +20; Rx mix net of lower fulfilment cost -15; co-payment cover and bonus recycling -30; wage inflation not absorbed and new-management investment -30; Swiss price cuts -5. Net +195bp.

Sensitivity, which shows where the forecast error will come from. In FY27, each 1% of revenue (about €39m) moves EBITDA by about €3.5m (2.5% of EBITDA) and EPS by about €0.14. Each 25bp of marketing or bonus spending moves EBITDA by about €10m (7%) and EPS by about €0.40. So management's choice of acquisition spend around the 2027 co-payment rise matters three times as much as a typical revenue miss. The Consumer should treat the marketing-plus-bonus ratio as the primary driver of the 18-month EBITDA cone, not revenue.

Cone and skew. FY27 EBITDA: €120-160m (3.1-4.0%), centred on €140m. Skew: mildly negative on margin (Sevenum commissioning in Q1 2027, co-payment cover, a possible investment message from the new chief executive at the 12 November Capital Markets Day), mildly positive on revenue.

Peer and precedent check. Apotea's EBIT margin fell from 5.3% (2021) to 1.0% (2022) during a capacity ramp and a price investment, then recovered to 5.2% in H1 2026 once the automated Varberg hub filled; operating leverage in online pharmacy works in both directions and the turning points are set by management's price and acquisition choices. DocMorris reached break-even mainly by cutting costs (marketing -310bp, CHF 15m AI-First programme), not by leverage. Meds Apotek (fulfilment 9.8% and marketing 10.5% of sales) shows the sub-scale version of the same cost structure.

Financial leverage

P-02

After assessing the financial leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share and the balance sheet over the next 18 months to four years?

C-603rd of 9

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: Financial leverage is a modest, fading amplifier of EPS and is not a solvency issue. Its importance lies elsewhere: the €300m convertible accretes in the accounts at 5.16% while paying 1.75% in cash, depressing reported EPS by about €0.35-0.45 a year relative to cash earnings, and its reported 2030 investor put sits inside the forecast horizon, which ties up the free cash flow of FY27-FY29 and makes buybacks before 2030 unlikely unless the shares approach the €173.74 conversion price.

The structure (company and AlphaSense report T-P4).

  • €300m senior unsecured convertible, due 16 April 2032; 1.75% cash coupon (€5.25m a year); redemption at 110% (€330m); conversion price €173.74 (1,726,718 shares at most, 8.4% of the share count); liability recognised at €259.3m at issue with an effective interest rate of 5.16%. An investor put in 2030 is referred to by AlphaSense; its terms must be confirmed (requested).
  • Residual €2.6m of the 2021/2028 convertible; the €13m Sevenum bank loan (EURIBOR plus 2.95%) amortises fully by 31 December 2026.
  • Lease liabilities €77.4m (end-2025).
  • Cash €119m (June 2026), after redeeming €64.5m of the 2028 convertible in January 2026.

Net finance cost, my forecast (P&L basis):

€m FY26 FY27 FY28 FY29 FY30
Convertible effective interest about -13.5 -14.4 -14.8 -15.3 -15.7
Lease interest -3.0 -3.0 -3.2 -3.3 -3.4
Redemption costs, bank loan, fees, other about -8.5 -1.6 -1.0 -1.0 -1.0
Interest income +3.0 +2.0 +3.0 +4.6 +6.5
Net financial items -22 -17 -16 -15 -13
of which non-cash accretion about -8 -9.0 -9.4 -9.9 -10.4

FY26 is inflated by the January 2026 redemption (H1 net finance cost was about €13.4m). The cash cost of the debt is only about €5m a year after FY26.

Effect on EPS. On my FY27 figures, EBIT excluding PPA of €74m less €17m of finance cost gives pre-tax profit of €57m, so financial gearing amplifies EBIT changes by about 1.3x (a 10% move in EBIT moves pre-tax profit by 13%). This falls to about 1.15x in FY28 and 1.1x by FY30. The first set's 1.9x was computed on consensus reported EBIT, which includes PPA; on the model's basis the amplification is smaller. Financial leverage therefore matters for the sign of reported net income in FY26-FY27, and very little thereafter.

The balance sheet path (net debt including leases and the convertible at book; my estimates):

€m, year-end FY26 FY27 FY28 FY29 FY30
Cash about 110 123 163 230 320
Convertible (book) 275 284 293 302 312
Leases and other debt about 78 77 75 75 75
Net debt about 243 238 205 147 67
Net debt / adjusted EBITDA 2.4x 1.7x 1.2x 0.7x 0.3x

Cash builds by free cash flow less about €5m a year of dividends to Galenica on its 49% of MediService.

The 2030 put, and why it matters for the model. If holders put the bonds in 2030 at an accreted value of roughly €315-325m, the cash of about €230m at end-FY29 would not quite cover it, so Redcare would need either FY30 free cash flow before the put date, a modest refinancing or a temporary drawdown. That is comfortably manageable, but it means the board is unlikely to start buybacks before 2030, which matches its stated policy of no returns "until the business reaches consistent structural net profitability". The alternative path is equity: if the shares trade above about €174 by 2030 (on my FY30 EPS of €5.7 on the model basis, that would need a P/E of about 30x), holders convert, 1.73m shares are issued (8.4% dilution), and about €310m of debt disappears, leaving the company with net cash of €250m or more. In IFRS diluted EPS the convertible stays anti-dilutive until basic EPS exceeds about €6.7 (the after-tax interest saved per new share), which my base case does not reach before FY31.

Comfort. High. No covenants are reported, nothing matures before the put, cash interest is about 3% of EBITDA from FY27, and net debt to EBITDA falls below 1x by FY29 on my numbers. The company's own net debt/EBITDA definition (1.1x at Q2 2026) excludes leases and probably uses the convertible's cash value net of cash; FactSet's (3.6x trailing) uses operating EBITDA. Neither is wrong; name the definition each time.

Peer contrast. DocMorris refinanced its 2028 convertible into a CHF 96.8-100m 2031 bond in September 2026 and still carries a CHF 200m bond maturing in 2029; its balance sheet constrains its marketing firepower more than Redcare's does. Apotea has near-zero net debt. Redcare's funding is therefore a competitive asset in a 2027 bonus contest, not a weakness.

5d

Cost-out scope NTM

2 answers
C-7766P-0360
C-77

How much of each major part of the cost base is cuttable or reducible?

A661st of 11

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: A meaningful slice of the cost base is discretionary. Relative to a 3% EBITDA margin it is large. Redcare has already shown it can cut marketing by 2 points while accelerating growth. The rest of the cost base is either regulated, variable with volume, or being automated.

By cost line (approximate share of revenue, my estimate where not disclosed):

  1. Cost of goods (about 78-80%). Not cuttable on Rx: the pharmacy margin is regulated and wholesale terms are statute-capped. Partly reducible on OTC through direct sourcing, own brands (faster than the group) and AI pricing. Perhaps 20-50bp of upside over three years.
  2. Marketing (about 5%; 5.1% in Q2 2026 against 7.2% a year earlier). Highly cuttable: it moved 210bp in one year. Every further 50bp of cut adds about €19-20m of EBITDA at FY27 revenue. It is the growth engine, though, and management targets about 5% as a steady state. Cutting below 4.5% would trade growth for margin.
  3. Fulfilment labour (warehouse and logistics personnel). Sevenum cuts labour cost per order by 70% from 2027 (Deutsche Bank: about 50bp of EBITDA, or "€10m of cost efficiencies" in 2027). Pilsen gives wage arbitrage. Moderately reducible.
  4. Shipping and carriers (about €3 per parcel). Barely cuttable. The signature rule adds €0.19 per Rx delivery. Parcel density and pick-up points help at the margin.
  5. IT, G&A and other overheads. Partly cuttable: entity mergers, AI in customer service, AI-First-style programmes. DocMorris' programme (CHF 15m savings, about 100 posts) is the peer template. A comparable programme at Redcare could yield perhaps €10-20m. This is conjecture; it is not announced.
  6. Payment fees and returns (0.7%). Low and not cuttable.

Total plausible reduction without harming growth: perhaps 80-150bp of revenue over three years, mostly from fulfilment labour and overheads. A further 50-100bp from marketing is available if management chooses margin over growth.

P-03

After assessing the scope for cost elimination and efficiency in greater detail, how much is likely to reach the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, and when?

A601st of 9

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: About €45-55m of annual EBITDA (roughly 100-110bp of FY30 revenue) is likely to come from identifiable efficiency over FY27-FY30: Sevenum (about €8m in FY27, €18m by FY28, €22m by FY29), a lower marketing ratio (about €12m in FY27 as the H2 2026 rate annualises, then about €5-9m a year from cohort layering), and smaller items. About half of the Sevenum saving is absorbed by its own depreciation below EBITDA. A broad overhead programme of the DocMorris "AI-First" kind is plausible under the new management but is not announced, so it is upside, not base case.

Lever by lever (my estimates of the year-on-year gain to EBITDA):

Lever FY27 FY28 FY29 FY30 Confidence
Sevenum automation, net of dual running in Q1 2027 +8 +10 +4 +2 Medium-high
Marketing ratio (5.3% FY26, 5.0% FY27, 4.8%, 4.7%, 4.6%) +12 +9 +5 +5 Medium
Pilsen wage arbitrage (non-Rx and Austria only) +1 +1 +1 0 Medium
Entity mergers and other simplification 0 0 0 0 High (no material effect, per the company)
Total identifiable about +21 about +20 about +10 about +7
Sevenum depreciation (below EBITDA) -9 -1 0 0 Medium

Sevenum, in detail. A €100m budget, on schedule and on budget per Deutsche Bank (18 September 2026), commissioning in Q1 2027, designed to double capacity and cut labour cost per order by 70%. Deutsche Bank estimates about €10m of efficiencies in 2027. I take €8m for FY27 (partial year, with two to three months of dual running and learning-curve costs), rising to about €18m in FY28 as volume moves onto the automated lines and about €22m by FY29. The project adds about €9-10m a year of depreciation (machinery is depreciated over five to ten years), so it is roughly neutral to EBIT in FY27 and adds about €8-12m a year to EBIT from FY28.

The commissioning risk is real and has a precedent. In 2021 the Sevenum migration, together with staff shortages, cut the sales outlook and Q3 2021 sales missed consensus by about 9%. Warehouse staff turnover was 39.1% in 2025. Apotea's Varberg hub added SEK 17.6m of depreciation in one quarter and ran at about 10,000 orders a day against capacity of 50,000, depressing margins for several quarters. I put the probability of a commissioning problem that costs a quarter at 20-25%; the cost would be about €3-8m of EBITDA and 2-4% of that quarter's revenue, concentrated in Q1 or Q2 2027. This is the main reason my Q1 2027 margin (2.8%) is well below my Q2 and Q3 2027 margins (4.0%).

Marketing. The move from 7.7% (H1 2025) to 5.6% (H1 2026) and 5.1% (Q2 2026) is largely done. Management says spending stabilises at about 5%. Two mechanical effects remain: the full-year effect of the H2 2026 rate (about 30bp in FY27), and cohort layering (90% of orders are repeat orders, so marketing as a share of revenue drifts down if acquisition spending is held flat; about 10-20bp a year). Apotea's 3% is not a realistic floor for Redcare, because Apotea holds about 45% of Swedish online pharmacy with heavy organic traffic and no bonus contest; I use a floor of about 4.5% by FY30. Note the trap from P-01: part of the 2026 "marketing saving" reappeared as Rx bonuses in gross margin, so the Consumer should track marketing plus bonuses together.

What cannot be cut. Rx cost of goods (purchase terms capped by statute), carrier rates (about €3 a parcel, plus €0.19 for the signature rule), and payment fees. The wholesale purchase conditions "under discussion" are a risk in the other direction: 25bp on about €1.4bn of Rx purchases is €3-4m.

The unannounced upside. DocMorris' AI-First programme targets at least CHF 15m a year (about 1.3% of its revenue) by FY27, has removed over 100 posts, and puts 75% of savings into EBITDA. A comparable programme at Redcare (customer service, content, pricing, administration) could plausibly yield €15-25m (0.4-0.6% of revenue). The new chief executive (marketplace background) and finance director (Amazon marketplace finance) are the kind of team that announces one; the Capital Markets Day on 12 November 2026 is the natural moment. I do not include it, because the more likely message from a marketplace chief executive is reinvestment in marketplace, retail media and AI, which would consume a similar amount.

When it reaches the line items. EBITDA: about 55% of the identifiable gain lands in FY27-FY28. EBIT: the FY27 gain is smaller (Sevenum depreciation), FY28 is the year cost savings reach EBIT in full. Cash: Sevenum capex ends in 2026 (about €30m in 2026), which is the larger cash effect: total capex falls from €119m (FY25) to about €86m (FY26) and about €72m (FY27).

5e

Reinforcing feedback loops

2 answers
C-4780P-0566
C-47To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?A801st of 11full answer under 4a Business quality
P-05

After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, such that dynamics at play today create additional scope for further positive/negative dynamics in the near/mid-term (reinforcing feedback loops), or the opposite (balancing loops), how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

A661st of 9

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

The Investment Analyst's heading After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

Judgement: The reinforcing loops (cohort layering and fixed-cost absorption) dominate through FY28 and are what make the 40-65bp a year margin rise forecastable. The balancing loops (competitive response, political response and working capital) are real but slow; their main effect is to cap the margin in the high-4s to low-5s by FY29-FY30 rather than to reverse the trajectory. The non-obvious point is that the reinforcing and balancing loops are coupled: the faster German Rx grows, the sooner the political and competitive balancing loops engage. That makes both the upside and the downside partly self-limiting, which narrows the four-year cone of EBITDA margins (about 4-5.5% in FY30) more than the cone of revenue.

The loops, ranked by their effect on the FY27-FY29 financials:

Loop Type Status now Effect on the line items (my estimate) What would change it
1. Cohort layering: repeat orders (90%), Rx cohorts worth 3.4-4x the revenue and 2-2.2x the gross profit of non-Rx cohorts, mixed orders rising to 40.8% Reinforcing Operating and measured Marketing ratio falls 10-20bp a year with flat acquisition spend; German Rx growth floor of about 15% from existing cohorts' frequency, basket and six-month repeats alone; non-Rx cross-sell Retention falling below 90%; no churn data is disclosed (the main gap)
2. Fixed-cost and automation: volume through Sevenum lowers cost per order, which funds bonuses that domestic pharmacies cannot legally pay, which brings volume Reinforcing Starts in Q1 2027 €8m (FY27) to €22m (FY29) of EBITDA; half recycled to patients over time Commissioning problems; utilisation below plan (Apotea's Varberg ran at 20% of capacity)
3. Competitive response: DocMorris recapitalised, break-even in Q3 2026, German Rx +78% Balancing Engaging Caps the share of the fee and Sevenum gains that Redcare keeps (about two thirds in my base); Redcare's share of online Rx drifts down from about 67% A full co-payment-cover war (15% probability, -€20-40m a year)
4. Political response: the more share online takes, the harder pharmacists lobby and the more insurers look for savings Balancing Latent No effect in my base before FY29; a widening of the downside cone in FY29-FY30 A bill restricting Rx bonuses (about 10% over three years) or a larger rebate (each €0.10 a pack is about €1.6-2.0m in FY28)
5. Working capital: Rx receivables from insurers take over a month, while OTC is paid at checkout Balancing Operating Working capital rises from 3.1% to 3.6% of revenue, absorbing about €20-22m a year (4-5% of incremental revenue) Wholesalers shortening payment terms (one week of payables is about €50m)
6. Monetisation: traffic raises retail media and marketplace revenue, which funds price and bonuses Reinforcing Weak, lumpy (Q1 2026 delays) About +10bp a year of margin A new management focus could double it; systems "failed to scale" in Q1 2026
7. Local pharmacy closures (below 16,600 in 2026) send chronic patients to choose a new provider Reinforcing Slow Supports acquisition; not separately modelled
8. Patient acquisition: active German Rx customers +60-70 thousand in Q2 2026, app downloads -18% year on year in September 2026 Neutral, softening My base: German Rx customers +15-18% a year PoPP and Health ID (Phase 2 from Q4 2027) could re-accelerate; a failed CardLink-to-PoPP hand-over in March 2028 could stall it

The coupling, and why it matters for the forecast. The fee step and Sevenum generate cash that Redcare can pay to patients (loop 2), which accelerates German Rx (loop 1), which raises online's share of a €55bn market from about 1.4% (Redcare alone, FY26) towards 2.5% (Redcare alone, FY30 in my base; about 3.5-4% for the whole online channel). That is the range at which experts and the first set expect pharmacists' and insurers' pressure to intensify (loop 4), and at which DocMorris has most to gain by contesting chronic cohorts (loop 3). So the bull case on German Rx revenue (say +35% in FY27) brings forward the policy and competitive response, and the bear case (+15%) defers it. The consequence for the Consumer is that revenue is the wider cone and margin the narrower one: I would put FY30 revenue at €4.6-6.0bn but the FY30 EBITDA margin at about 4-5.5%.

How the loops shape the line items.

  • Revenue: growth decays smoothly (13%, 11.5%, 10.5%, 10%) rather than accelerating; the reinforcing loops sustain German Rx at 16-27% a year, but no S-curve acceleration is in my base because acquisition indicators are softening and PoPP is a transition, not yet a catalyst.
  • Gross margin percentage: falls 20-30bp a year (mix), partly recycled bonuses.
  • Operating costs: fall about 50-70bp a year as a share of revenue (loops 1 and 2).
  • EBITDA margin: rises 40-65bp a year, decelerating as the balancing loops engage.
  • Working capital and free cash flow: loop 5 absorbs about a quarter to a third of EBITDA growth in FY27 and about a sixth by FY30.

Precedents. Apotea and Sweden show loops 1 and 2 running for a decade (online at 27% of the Swedish market, Apotea at 10.6% of the total), with the balancing loop showing up as price competition (Apotea's 2022 margin collapse) rather than policy. Zur Rose/DocMorris shows loop 2 running in reverse: sub-scale fixed costs, cash burn, the sale of its profitable Swiss business (CHF 360m to Migros, 2023) and dilutive raises. Chewy shows how far loop 1 can go when repeat ordering is automated (Autoship at 84.6% of sales).

Guidance is deliberately conservative and moderately specific. It is built on revenue blocks and named margin levers by a finance director whose explicit mandate is credibility.

The pattern since March 2026 is "rebase, then raise revenue in small ad hoc steps, hold margin":

  • FY26 revenue guidance raised twice;
  • margin held at 2.5-3.0% against a running 3.5%;
  • consensus at the top of every range;
  • the mid-term 5% target above consensus (4.2% for FY28) and, in my view, achievable on enacted and committed levers.

Informal commentary under-promised on H2 Rx and is confident on mid-term margin.

For the model: treat FY26 guidance as a floor (EBITDA €100-108m). Treat the mid-term target as realistic (4.5-5% by FY28). Expect the CMD to add EPS and cash framing, which will need the below-EBITDA blocks the company has never guided. The main guidance risk is a new chief executive re-weighting towards investment at the CMD.

Item Guidance Consensus

Why guidance sits below.

  • The new finance director's stated "highly prudent forecasting philosophy to rebuild capital market credibility" after the March reset.
  • An explicit lapping of the Q4 2025 Rx bonus launch.
  • Uncertainty over Q4 OTC and flu.
  • Room for promotional investment around the co-payment rise.

  • Formal guidance comes with FY26 results (March 2027). The first indication may come at the CMD (12 November 2026).
  • Consensus: revenue €3,894m (+12.7%), EBITDA €138m (3.5%), EPS €1.32.
  • Management's practice is EBITDA guidance one year ahead and a shift to EPS.
  • Expect an initial FY27 guide in March 2027 with a margin range around consensus or slightly below, and growth "low double digits" (Deutsche Bank's view).

Mid-term. The 5% by FY28 sits above consensus (4.2%). The sell-side discounts management's mid-term ambition after the 8%-to-5% cut. The mechanical levers (Fixum, Sevenum, marketing) support closing much of the gap.

C-71

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

A701st of 11

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: Conservative on both revenue and margin, once adjusted for stronger input forces in Rx and the enacted fee tailwind.

Revenue.

  • FY26 guidance of 16-18% sits against the last five years' 9.5%, 13.6%, 49.4% (with MediService), 31.8% and 24.1%.
  • The input forces are stronger now in Rx (e-Rx share and acceleration, six-month cycles, co-payments, CardLink certainty) and weaker in OTC (saturated, soft consumer).
  • The implied Q4 growth of 8-15% against Q3 at about 20%, and German Rx Q4 at about +17-37% against September's +51%, is conservative even after lapping the bonus launch.

Margin.

  • The 2.5-3.0% FY26 range sits against Q2 and Q3 at about 3.5% and a Q4 2025 base of about 1.6%. It requires only a weak Q4.
  • The mid-term 5% by FY28 sits against a current run-rate of 3.5%. The enacted and committed levers alone add about 80-120bp by 2027-28:
  • Fixum net €9.5-13.3m in 2027, about 25-35bp;
  • Sevenum about 50bp;
  • fixed-cost absorption on 12-15% growth, perhaps 30-50bp.
  • Against that, Rx mix dilutes gross margin (50-100bp by FY28) and co-payment cover costs money.
  • Net: about 4.5-5% by FY28 looks achievable, so the target is moderately conservative to fair. The previous 8% target was not credible; 5% is.

Long-term (above 8%). Not conservative; it needs Apotea-like maturity.

Precedent. In 2025 the company set ambitious targets (above 25% growth) and missed slightly. The 2026 philosophy is the deliberate opposite. The sell-side's 2026 revisions (FY27 EBITDA +14% in six months) are the market slowly learning that.

C-73

What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

A601st of 11

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: Five features are material for modelling and for reading the stock.

  1. EBITDA guidance is given only for the next year, and the company is moving its focus to EPS (announced at FY25 results). That is unusual for a company whose EPS is still negative. It implies management expects EPS to become the cleaner signal once interest, tax and depreciation stabilise. It also means FY27 EBITDA guidance will be the last of its type after March 2027 or so.
  2. Preliminary quarterly sales updates have ended. One full update per quarter, plus the January trading statement. Fewer price-moving events, but more weight on the monthly gematik and app-tracker data published by brokers, which become the de facto real-time KPIs.
  3. Ad hoc guidance raises before quarterly reports (15 June, 28 September). Redcare uses ad hoc notices when trading exceeds guidance, which signals a strict disclosure culture (Dutch and German market-abuse rules) and gives positive surprises intra-quarter.
  4. The CMD on 12 November 2026 is two weeks after Q3 results and three weeks into a new chief executive's tenure. Expect new mid-term targets, perhaps on EPS and free cash flow, a new segment disclosure (marketplace and retail media) and possibly German Rx KPIs. It is high-impact and two-sided.
  5. Disclosure conflicts that the Consumer should resolve with investor relations (head of investor relations: Irina Zhurba): - German Rx quarterly revenue (€180m against Jefferies' €205m); - active Rx customer counts; - working capital definitions; - the gross margin basis; - net debt definitions (1.1x against FactSet's 3.6x).

Also notable: a short-interest base partly made up of convertible arbitrage, and broker coverage concentrated in two lead brokers (Deutsche Bank's Jan Koch, Jefferies' Martin Comtesse), whose monthly trackers dominate sell-side flow. Smaller domestic brokers (mwb, Metzler, MP Capital, Oddo BHF) update after events.

  • That leaves room for another 5-15% of EBITDA upgrades, amplified into larger EPS upgrades.

6c

Case simplicity

7 answers
Judgement56C-7960D-0266D-0345D-0452D-0558D-0657
Group judgement

Risks

–56

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts for the next 18 months are predictable in direction, moderately predictable in size at EBITDA, and weakly predictable at EPS.

Direction is well supported. The earlier sets cohere on what drives the business; Q3 confirmed the volume engine (German Rx +56%, customer additions doubled); the fee is law; the automation is installed; and about three quarters of the forecast improvement rests on strengths already shown.

Size is limited by concentration and gearing, not by aggressive forecasts. The forecasts of record equal consensus at EBITDA, so the danger is not that they are high. It is that the margin turns on three nodes (the commercial give-back, Sevenum and the state), each of which has caused a miss before, and that EPS is a geared claim on roughly one percentage point of margin above fixed charges. The cone for FY27 is about €114m to €158m of EBITDA and about €1.2 to €2.9 of model-basis EPS, with a mildly negative skew.

Timing helps. The uncertainty is front-loaded: the co-payment regime and the Sevenum go-live both fall in Q1 2027 and will be in reported numbers by early May 2027. H2 2027, when the fee stops helping, is the first clean test of whether the margin rises on its own. A second, smaller knot sits at the end of the window, at the CardLink sunset of 31 March 2028.

Tracking is good for volume and poor for the give-back, and the share price, which moves almost wholly on company news, will react to proxies in the meantime.

Three steers for the memo.

  1. Present FY27 as a range, and say what sets it. An investor should hear "3.0% to 3.9%, decided mainly by how much Redcare and DocMorris hand to patients after 1 January", not a point estimate.
  2. Describe the risk as the repeat of known failure modes. Promotions in a soft quarter, a logistics transition, and a change of rule: all three are on the calendar in the next five months, under a chief executive who has not yet faced any of them at Redcare. His stated priorities, and the conservative habits of the finance director, are the evidence that the response will differ this time; Q4 2026 is the first test.
  3. Do not use the model's return table or its EPS comparison with consensus as they stand (D-07 and I-02).

On the memo's lifecycle question, I would place the stock as turning up, with the thesis beginning to broaden from "Rx volume" to "profit and cash", and with delivery about to be tested before the broadening can be called secure.

C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

C604th of 11

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: The perimeter is moderately narrow and stable. One regulatory regime (German statutory Rx) drives most of the value and the growth. That makes predictability high on direction and concentrated in policy risk.

Countries. Seven: Germany, Austria and Switzerland (82% of FY25 revenue: €2,401m), plus Belgium, France, Italy and the Netherlands (18%: €540m). Germany alone is probably above 60% of revenue: German Rx about €745m plus German OTC.

Business lines:

  • German Rx: about 22% of FY26 revenue, but the majority of growth and of incremental profit.
  • Swiss Rx through the 51% MediService: about 18%; low margin.
  • DACH non-Rx (OTC, beauty, supplements): about 42%.
  • International (mostly non-Rx): about 18%.
  • Marketplace and retail media: under 5%, unsegmented.

Change. Little. Entity simplification; no exits; adjacencies are product-level, not new countries.

Predictability:

  • Positive. The perimeter is understandable. Rx volume is visible monthly in gematik data. The fee schedule is enacted.
  • Negative. Concentration on one country's pharmacy policy, plus OTC exposure to consumer and weather.
  • Currency is minor (CHF about 19% of revenue, naturally hedged).

Compared with CVS (two countries, three divisions) or Georg Fischer (cyclical, multi-end-market), Redcare is simpler. It has a single policy node.

D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

–66

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: The expected change over the next 18 months is plausible, and about three quarters of it rests on strengths the business has already shown or on law already enacted. The part that rests on things not yet shown is small in euros but sits exactly where the company's record is weakest: commissioning a warehouse without a stumble, holding a margin gain for more than one year, and earning money outside the DACH region. The thesis does not need the two capabilities the company most visibly lacks, retail media at scale and a telemedicine funnel.

The expected change. Revenue growth of about 13% in FY27; a margin rising from 2.9% (FY26) to 3.55% (FY27) and 4.0% (FY28); the first profitable year and the first positive free cash flow.

What it rests on, and whether each has been shown.

What the forecast needs Shown? Evidence
German Rx revenue of about €960-985m in FY27 (+27% to +30%) Yes It needs only the 2026 pace to continue: revenue rose €12m from Q1 to Q2 and €17m from Q2 to Q3; about €12-15m a quarter from here delivers it. No acceleration from PoPP is assumed
Repeat ordering that lets marketing fall as a share of revenue Yes 90% repeat orders; in Q3 revenue rose 17.7% on flat marketing spending while 123,000 Rx customers were added
The fee steps Enacted €9.00 from July 2026 and €9.50 from January 2027; the DACH margin rose from about 4.0% to 4.2% from Q2 to Q3, which retains roughly two thirds of the first step
Capital spending below 2% of revenue; funding Yes Sevenum spending of €19m to September and €10m in Q4 is on budget; cash of €132m; nothing due before the 2030 put
Conservative guidance, delivered Yes, for one year Two raises in 2026 under this finance director; the nine-month margin sits at the top of the range
Sevenum savings of €8m in FY27 and €18m in FY28 Not yet Installed and testing well; but the 2021 migration cost about 9% of a quarter's sales, Pilsen disrupted Austrian supply, and warehouse staff turnover is 39%
International margin of +1.0% in FY27 Not yet −0.4% in Q3 after +0.9% in Q2; −€2.7m over nine months against the second set's small profit for FY26
Retail media and marketplace adding about 10bp a year Not yet Grew more slowly than revenue in Q3; systems "failed to scale" in Q1
A margin that rises three years running Never −0.3%, 3.0%, 1.4%, 2.0%, then 2.9% (FY26): each gain so far has been followed by a relapse chosen by management (the promotions of late 2024 and of Q4 2025)
Restraint in the 2027 co-payment contest Partly Marketing discipline held through 2026, but Redcare raised its new-customer bonus in Q3 to match DocMorris

The proportion. Of the increase in EBITDA from FY26 to FY28 (about €75m), the gross drivers before cost inflation and give-backs sum to roughly €105m. About €80m of that is shown or enacted: today's margin earned on the extra revenue, fixed costs spread over more volume, the marketing ratio and the fee. About €27m is to be shown: Sevenum (€18m) and retail media and own brands (about €9m). Seen by segment, the forecast also needs International to improve by about €9m. So roughly three quarters of the gross improvement relies on what the business has, and what it most visibly lacks is not needed.

Three points that the Consumer should weigh.

  1. The DACH segment already earns more than the group is forecast to earn in FY28. DACH was at 4.2% in Q3; the group base case is 4.0% in FY28. The forecast therefore needs DACH to hold a little above today's level through the co-payment contest and International to stop losing money. That is a low bar for the core and a real one for the periphery.
  2. Persistence, not level, is the capability to be acquired. The company has reached a 3% margin before (FY23) and lost it within a year by choice. What is different now is structural, not behavioural: a higher fee, a larger repeat-ordering base and an owner-led board that has moved the stated objective to earnings and cash. Whether the behaviour has changed will be seen in Q4 2026, the first promotional quarter under the new chief executive. Guidance leaves him room for a Q4 margin anywhere from 1.4% to 3.4%.
  3. The precedents say "achieved, but late". Apotea needed four years to regain a 5% margin after its 2022 fall and a new hub. The AlphaSense precedent report concludes that Redcare's 5% target is most likely reached 6 to 12 months late. The second set's 4.0% in FY28 and 5% around FY30 sits at the cautious end of the evidence; a range of 3.6-4.6% for FY28 is what the evidence supports.

Free cash flow. Positive free cash flow in FY27 is plausible but thinly covered in the base case (about €10m in the model, €18m on a cash basis). One week of supplier payment terms is about €50m. I would put the chance of a positive figure on the model's definition at about 60-65%.

D-03In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?–45full answer under 2a Internal risks
D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

–52

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: The cone is narrow for revenue (about ±3%), moderate for EBITDA (about ±13% around €139m for FY27) and wide for EPS (about −45% to +35%). The skew is mildly negative in size, not in probability: the adverse events are larger and faster than the favourable ones. Half of the uncertainty resolves by May 2027. The case is predictable in direction and only moderately predictable in size, and the size that the memo's return rests on, EPS, is the least predictable line.

The cone for FY27 (my figures; the second set's scenarios, re-weighted for what Q3 showed).

Bear (22%) Base (58%) Bull (20%)
What happens German Rx +15%; a partial co-payment contest; a Sevenum quarter lost; non-Rx +5% As forecast German Rx +35%; Sevenum at management's 50bp; retail media scales
Revenue about €3.80bn €3.93bn about €4.03bn
Adjusted EBITDA; margin about €114m; 3.0% €139m; 3.55% about €158m; 3.9%
EPS, model basis about €1.2 about €2.2 about €2.9
EPS, reported-like about €0.55 about €1.55 about €2.3

The probability-weighted EBITDA is about €137m, in line with consensus (€138m).

Why I moved the weights only slightly. Since the second set, three things narrowed the left side: the chief executive's message was profit and cash, not reinvestment; Sevenum is installed and testing well (I put the chance of a lost quarter at 15-20%, against the second set's 20-25%); and customer additions doubled. Three things held it open: International fell back into loss, the first broker changes after Q3 were downward, and a co-payment waiver ban is now a named initiative. The net effect is a slightly lower weight on the bear case, not a narrower cone.

The skew. The bear case sits €25m below the base and the bull case €19m above. More important is speed: a co-payment contest or a commissioning problem appears within one quarter, whereas retail media, cost programmes and faster conversion of patients accrue over several.

Tails outside the cone, inside the 18 months.

  • A full co-payment-cover contest: about 15%; it would cost €20-40m a year.
  • A co-payment waiver ban in force: 5% or less within 18 months, though a draft bill or ministry statement, which would move the shares, is far more likely (I would say one chance in three). My three-year probability for a restriction on bonuses or waivers is 15-20%, against the first set's 10%, because an instrument and a sponsor now exist and because the 2027 co-payment rise makes the waiver both more valuable to patients and more visible to politicians.
  • A stumble at the CardLink sunset on 31 March 2028: 5-10%. It would fall in the last quarter of the window and into the FY28 guidance. The research on Health ID shows real friction for patients over 55 (a PIN by post, identity checks, recent phone software), and about 90% of Redcare's e-prescription redemptions come through the app.
  • A larger rebate or a fee clawback from the €42.3bn savings agenda: perhaps 5-8% within the window. No broker has analysed it.
  • On the right: a pull of patients online from the co-payment rise that exceeds the forecast. German Rx needs only the 2026 pace, so the upside here is real.

The time structure of the cone. The two events that decide FY27, the co-payment regime from 1 January and the Sevenum go-live, will both be about two months old when the company gives its first FY27 guidance on 3 March 2027, and will be visible in reported margins with the Q1 results in early May 2027. Before then the cone cannot narrow on company data. After July 2027, with two quarters reported, the FY27 cone should be about half as wide. The 18 months are therefore two periods: six months of low visibility on the decisive variables, then twelve of much higher visibility, with a second, smaller knot of uncertainty at the end (PoPP and the CardLink sunset).

How this compares with the past. The company's own guided margin range has narrowed from three points (FY22) to half a point (FY26), and quarterly margins in 2026 ran from 1.7% to 3.5%. Apotea's margin moved four points in a single year (2021 to 2022). Against that history a cone of 3.0% to 3.9% for FY27 is tight; against a typical defensive healthcare distributor it is wide.

The share price cone is far wider than the fundamental cone. Jefferies' own scenarios span €35 to €120 around a €97 target. The first set's fair trough of €45-55 stands.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

–58

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: Helpful for volume, weak for the variable that decides the next six months. The company now discloses enough each quarter to track German Rx momentum, customer additions and the marketing ratio, and third parties publish monthly data. But the cost of Rx bonuses and co-payment cover is undisclosed and sits inside the gross margin, and the calendar gives no reported margin under the new co-payment regime until early May 2027, the very end of the six months. The case can be monitored. Its decisive margin drivers will have to be monitored through proxies, and the most-watched proxy has just pointed the wrong way.

What the company gives each quarter (all of it new or improved in 2026).

  • German Rx revenue in euros, and the guidance for it.
  • Active German Rx customers (1.67m, 1.73m, then about 1.85-1.90m in Q1 to Q3).
  • The marketing ratio (5.1% in Q2, 4.9% in Q3).
  • The gross margin, with a bridge given on the call (Rx mix, non-Rx pricing, retail media).
  • Adjusted EBITDA for DACH and International.
  • Repeat rate and NPS; Sevenum progress and spending; a cash bridge.

What others give monthly.

  • gematik's count of e-prescriptions (48.3m in September).
  • Jefferies' tracker of app downloads and active users, early each month.
  • The trade press (Apotheke Adhoc), which reports bonus offers and political initiatives before brokers do.
  • From 1 January 2027, the co-payment offers themselves, which are public in the two companies' apps.
  • DocMorris' own reporting, which is the best read on the rival's intent.

What is missing, in order of importance.

  1. The cost of Rx bonuses and co-payment cover. It is deducted from revenue and never shown. It is the main component of the commercial give-back, which sets the margin cone (D-03).
  2. Reported margins in time. See the calendar below.
  3. Packs, and gross profit per pack. The whole external chain runs per pack (D-06), yet packs must be inferred from revenue.
  4. Retention by cohort. Repeat orders are given as a share of orders, which cannot show a cohort decaying.
  5. Retail media and marketplace revenue. Unsegmented; known only through remarks such as "grew below the top line".
  6. Anything below EBITDA, although the company is moving its message to EPS.
  7. A strategy day. Redcare has none scheduled (reading notes).

The calendar of the six months, and what each date can tell us.

Date Event What it can show
12 November 2026 DocMorris' Capital Markets Day How much of the higher co-payment the rival will cover, and its 2027 targets
Early December, January Monthly trackers Whether downloads keep falling while customer additions hold
1 January 2027 Co-payment rise; fee to €9.50; rebate to €2.07 The offers in both apps: first order only, or all repeat orders
11 January 2027 Redcare's trading update Q4 and FY26 revenue and German Rx; probably no margin
3 March 2027 FY26 results and first FY27 guidance The Q4 margin (the first promotional quarter under the new chief executive); the FY27 ranges; two months of experience of the new regime and of Sevenum, in management's words only
Early May 2027 Q1 2027 results The first reported margin under the new regime and the first Sevenum quarter

So for four of the six months the two decisive variables, the give-back after 1 January and the Sevenum go-live, are visible only through proxies and management's words.

The proxy that misled. App downloads fell 18% year on year in September and 32% in October, and both earlier sets, like Jefferies, read this as slowing acquisition. In the same quarter the company added 123,000 German Rx customers, twice the previous quarter's number. Downloads measure new installations against a year-earlier period inflated by the bonus launch; customers can be added from the large installed base. The share price nonetheless reacts to the tracker each month. The Consumer should rank the company's quarterly customer additions and German Rx revenue above downloads, and treat a tracker-driven fall in the shares as noise unless active users also fall.

The single most useful measure the Consumer can build from what is disclosed is the gross margin less the marketing ratio. It nets out any movement of acquisition cost between the two lines, which is exactly what the co-payment contest may cause. It was 16.6% in Q2 2025 and 17.0% in Q2 2026; 16.2% in Q3 2025 and 16.9% in Q3 2026. Rx mix lowers the gross margin by about 50bp a year at the forecast growth, only part of which returns through a lower marketing ratio, so a flat reading is in fact a modest improvement. In Q1 2027 the fee alone should add about 30bp. If the measure falls by more than about 50bp year on year in Q1 2027, the contest is costing more than the base case allows.

Other triggers to carry.

  • German Rx revenue rising by less than €10m from one quarter to the next (the base case needs €12-15m).
  • Fewer than 80,000 German Rx customers added in a quarter.
  • A Q4 2026 margin below 2.5%, which would say the old habit of buying growth in Q4 has survived the change of management.
  • Any draft of the digital health act that contains the co-payment waiver ban.
  • Any statement that the Sevenum go-live has moved out of Q1.
D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

–57

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: The trace is clear from volume to revenue, serviceable from the named levers to EBITDA with one opaque link, and poor from EBITDA to EPS, where the company guides nothing and the brokers do not share a definition. The external transmission, from the state's fee and co-payment rules to profit per pack and so to EPS, is unusually clear for a consumer business and can be computed. The oddity of this company is that we know to the cent what the state gives it per pack and do not know what it gives back to the patient per pack.

The internal chain.

What the company says Driver Line it lands on How clear
Revenue +16-18%; German Rx €730-760m; non-Rx +10-12% Rx customers times revenue per customer; non-Rx orders Revenue, with German Rx disclosed each quarter Clear. Packs and revenue per pack are not published
Margin 2.5-3.0%; marketing "about 5%" Marketing spending against a growing repeat base Selling and distribution; the ratio is disclosed Clear
Sevenum: labour cost per order down 70%; "about 50bp in 2027" Automated fulfilment Fulfilment costs inside selling and distribution, not shown separately; depreciation rises below EBITDA Partly clear. Whether 50bp is the 2027 effect or the later run-rate is not stated, and the depreciation that offsets about half of it at operating profit is not quantified
The fee schedule State-set fee and rebate per pack Gross profit Clear per pack; needs a pack count
Rx bonuses and co-payment cover "adjusted dynamically" Price paid to the patient Deducted from revenue Opaque. This is the break in the chain. DocMorris has booked part of its equivalent in marketing, so the two cannot be compared
Marketplace and retail media "in their infancy" Fees from suppliers Revenue and gross margin, unsegmented Opaque
"Positive free cash flow in FY27" Lower capital spending; working capital Cash flow Clear in direction; the definition (before or after leases) is not stated in my files
A move to EPS as the headline None given Depreciation, amortisation of acquired intangibles (€12.8m, annual report only), interest (5.16% effective against a 1.75% coupon, in the notes), tax (€311.9m of unrecognised Dutch losses, usable against only half of profit above €1m), minorities Poor. Brokers reach €0.84 to €2.04 for FY27 from the same EBITDA

The external chain, which can be computed.

  • The fee. In 2027 a pack earns €9.50 plus 3% of the purchase price, less a rebate of €2.07: about €8.8 of gross profit on about €60 of revenue. Each €0.10 a pack is about €1.6m of EBITDA and about €0.06 of EPS in FY27 (about 16m packs).
  • Volume. Each additional million packs brings about €5-6m of contribution after fulfilment, payment and an average bonus: about €0.2 of EPS.
  • The co-payment. The law sets it at €7.50 to €15 from January 2027. Covering a further €2.50 on a quarter of all packs would cost about €10m, about €0.4 of EPS. That is the scale of the decision management takes in January.
  • The rails. CardLink to 31 March 2028 and PoPP Phase 2 from Q4 2027 are dated by gematik; about 90% of e-prescription redemptions run through the app.
  • Where it is not clear. Non-Rx, the other half of gross profit, responds to weather, consumer confidence and price-comparison sites. October's "below 10%" start shows how little warning there is.

Why the mismatch matters. A change by the state can be turned into an EPS effect within a day, which makes the regulatory risk measurable even though its timing is not predictable. A change in the company's own commercial stance cannot be measured until the quarter after it happens, and then only as a residual in the gross margin. The first kind of risk is the larger in a tail event and the second is the larger in an ordinary year.

What would repair the chain. Three disclosures: packs, bonus cost per pack, and guidance below EBITDA. A first FY27 guidance framed on EPS, which the finance director has signalled, would force the third and would also shrink the spread of broker EPS.

C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

F329th of 11

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: Perception risk is high. The stock has a record of 15-60% swings on sentiment alone, and its profile matches what the current market regime sells.

  1. Expectation risk after a 62.6% six-month rally, at the two events immediately ahead: Q3 results (29 October 2026) and the new chief executive's CMD (12 November 2026). A cautious margin framing, a "re-investment year" message or a weak Q4 outlook (guidance implies Q4 growth of 8-15%) could trigger a sharp fall even with good fundamentals.
  2. Market regime (Fitness for Fighting). Risk-off is confirmed and broadening: US 10-year near 5.10%, Brent at $111, French and Italian sovereign stress. The Momentum tilt is Underweight; the Fragility tilt is a Double Underweight (a loss-making, convertible-financed, high-beta mid-cap). A momentum unwind would hit Redcare whatever its upgrades. FfF also flags that a de-rated AI complex recovering would be funded by selling this year's leaders.
  3. Monthly data trackers. Jefferies' gematik and app-user trackers move the stock monthly. August's -16% month-on-month dip in scripts, and app users falling 3%, show how seasonal noise can be read as a structural stall. The Risks report's review trigger is a sequential fall in monthly active users of more than 10%.
  4. Competitor and sector events. - DocMorris news: its Q3 beat already re-rated it to 0.59x EV/sales; a DocMorris promotional campaign would be read as a price war. - Rossmann's launch: the dm entry in December 2024 took 25% off Redcare's shares despite no operational impact. - Apothekertag or ministerial statements on bonuses or bans. - Galenica or Apotea results.
  5. Positioning. Short interest of about 17% (part convertible hedging). Index exclusion from the STOXX Europe 600 (September 2025). Liquidity of only USD 12-18m a day traded. Squeezes and air pockets are both possible.
  6. Leadership headlines. The CEO change took more than 15% off in a day. A further senior departure (for example Holler) or a commercial director appointment that disappoints would be read as instability.
  7. A broker consensus that is too uniform. Buy share is 67% (FactSet) with targets of €71-140. Any downgrade from a lead broker (Deutsche Bank €104, Jefferies €97) after the rally would be amplified.

Precedent for magnitude: the shares fell from above €100 to about €30 (March 2026) in about a year, then more than doubled.

Track record (guidance raise/sell-side upgrades) ~L2-3Y

C-61How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?D626th of 11full answer under 4b Management quality
C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

B+682nd of 11

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: Under the current finance director the behaviour is "rebase low, raise in small steps, never raise margin until it is in the bag". That is the sandbagging-precision archetype in the Wisdom Library. The new chief executive has no upgrade record at Redcare.

Record:

  • 2026:
  • March: a reset well below expectations, with the mid-term target cut from 8% to 5%.
  • 15 June: an ad hoc raise of revenue growth to 15-17% and German Rx to €680-720m, made on quarter-to-date trading before the Q2 report.
  • 28 September: an ad hoc raise to 16-18% and €730-760m.
  • Margin unchanged at 2.5-3.0% both times, despite Q2 and Q3 at about 3.5%.
  • 2023: initial non-Rx guidance met; total sales +49% "fully achieving the company's revised upward targets". So raises happened in 2023 too.
  • 2022: all initial guidance elements met (non-Rx +17.2% against 15-25%; margin -0.3% against -1.5% to +1.5%).
  • 2025: growth of 24.1% against a target above 25% (a slight miss); margin of 2.0% at the low end; no raise.
  • Earlier: 2018 and 2021 forecasts cut.

Nature.

  • Upgrades are revenue-led, small (about 1 point of growth) and ad hoc, triggered by trading. That is clear evidence of conservative initial ranges.
  • Margin is held, with gross profit "reinvested in marketing" (Jefferies) or held as a buffer against competition (Deutsche Bank).
  • The company is ending preliminary quarterly sales updates, so future upgrades will come with full quarterly reports or as ad hoc notices.

Behaviour of the chief executive. Heinrich's upgrades in 2026 came alongside Krampe. Schmid von Linstow inherits a guidance philosophy he co-approved as a director.

Implication for the model. Treat guidance as a floor. Expect further small revenue beats, and a margin outturn at or slightly above the top of ranges.

C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

D567th of 11

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: The record is mixed over five years, improving over three, and strong over the last two quarters. Misses cluster in OTC-soft, promotion-heavy or logistics-transition periods. Beats come when Rx momentum and cost discipline coincide, as now.

Last 1 year:

  • Q4 2025 and FY25 disappointed: growth of 24.1% against a target above 25%; margin of 2.0% at the low end; Q4 margin compressed to about 1.6% by bonus campaigns. FY26 targets set in March 2026 were "well below expectations" (Jefferies). Deutsche Bank cut adjusted EBITDA forecasts by 23%: a "painful expectations reset".
  • Then Q1 2026 was solid. Q2 2026 beat consensus adjusted EBITDA by 26% (€29.6-30m against €23.5m) and revenue by about €8m (1%). Q3 2026 preliminary showed German Rx +56%, a flat margin around 3.5% and a second raise.
  • Net: one large reset followed by two beats and raises.

Last 3 years:

  • FY23: guidance met or exceeded; revised-up targets achieved.
  • FY24: strong growth (31.8%), though the margin fell to 1.4% on promotional spend in late 2024. Morgan Stanley's equal-weight stance reflected that marketing pressure.
  • FY25: slight miss.
  • The pattern: revenue usually delivered; margin often below early hopes.

Last 5 years:

  • 2021: Q2 growth of 7.3% (a miss), an outlook cut on the Sevenum migration and a bonus ban, Q3 sales about 9% below consensus.
  • 2022: met all initial guidance elements.
  • 2018: forecast cut on German price competition.

Comparable environments.

  • Present conditions: a favourable regulatory tailwind, rational competition, soft OTC and a major logistics project, on top of a new chief executive.
  • The closest analogue for a logistics transition in peak season is 2021, when Redcare missed.
  • The closest analogue for regulatory tailwind plus discipline is 2023 and 2026, when it beat.

Risk for H1 2027. Sevenum commissioning in Q1 2027, plus co-payment competition, plus a new chief executive, together resemble 2021 more than 2026. The Consumer should not extrapolate the 2026 beat streak mechanically into Q1 2027.

Lean revenue up, because this finance director guides revenue conservatively and German Rx has beaten every guide in 2026. Lean the FY27 margin neutral (conservative guidance against a Q1 2027 that resembles 2021). Lean the mid-term margin below the company's target, which I have already done by putting 5% in FY30 rather than FY28.

Big projects Sevenum 2021 migration caused a sales miss of about 9%; Pilsen live late 2025 with an early Austrian supply disruption fixed by early 2026; Sevenum automation on schedule and budget Delivered, with a transition cost each time
Sell-side estimates FY27 EPS consensus -57% over 12 months, then +85% over six months The estimate base itself is unstable; do not anchor on it

Weights I attach to my forecasts (the plausible range around my central figures):

FY26 FY27 FY28 FY29-FY30
Revenue ±1% (high weight) ±3% (high-medium) ±6% (medium) ±10% (low-medium)
Adjusted EBITDA ±5% ±12% ±18% ±25%
EPS, model basis ±25% ±30% ±30% ±35%
Free cash flow ±€15m ±€20m ±€30m ±€40m

Direction of lean, and why.

  1. Revenue: up by about 1-2% against my central case for FY26-FY27. Three successive under-promises on German Rx, a Q4 2026 implied growth of 6-14% that sits well below September's +51% in German Rx, and a finance director whose explicit mandate is credibility. My FY26 of €3,473m already sits at the top of the guided €3.41-3.47bn; a figure of €3.48-3.50bn would not surprise me.
  2. FY27 EBITDA margin: neutral. Against a conservative guide sit three features that resemble 2021 rather than 2026: Sevenum commissioning in the peak flu quarter with 39% warehouse turnover, the co-payment contest from 1 January, and a new chief executive who may frame 2027 as an investment year at the 12 November Capital Markets Day (I put about 35% on a message that trims the FY27 margin by 20-40bp against consensus). Q1 has also been the weakest-margin quarter in each of the last two years (Q1 2026 margins were "again below expectations", Jefferies, 6 May 2026). Do not extrapolate the 2026 beat streak into Q1 2027.
  3. FY28-FY30 margin: below the company, neutral against my figures. The company's 5% by FY28 needs the 2026 rate of improvement to continue; the decomposition in P-01 shows it cannot at the same size.
  4. Below EBITDA: wide, two-sided, low weight. The company has never guided D&A, finance cost, tax or minorities, has a 41.2% H1 2026 tax rate from deferred tax on convertible repurchases, and will at some point recognise a large deferred tax asset. These items move reported EPS by more than operations do in FY26-FY27.

Peer cross-check. DocMorris has raised its 2026 guidance twice and reached break-even early, which shows the market tailwind is broad; but its record over 2021-24 (repeated misses, capital raises) is a reminder that a favourable channel shift does not protect forecasts from execution. Apotea delivered its margin recovery largely as guided once automation was in place.

Forecasts and upgrades

7

Forecasts with good growth rates and higher growth/margins than past

–not yet rated

Memo topic 6, : the Rater's score

7a

Growth rate

4 answers
Judgement64C-4960P-0856P-1064
Group judgement

Financial forecasts

A641st of 9

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Redcare's forecasts for the next four years describe a business crossing from growth bought with losses to growth that compounds earnings and cash. The direction is unusually forecastable, because most of the improvement is mechanical: chronic Rx cohorts that reorder 90% of the time, a state-set fee that steps up in January 2027, an automated hub commissioning in Q1 2027, capex falling below 2% of revenue, and below-EBIT costs that are now small and stable. My base case has revenue of €3.94bn in FY27 and €5.32bn in FY30, adjusted EBITDA of €140m and €258m (3.55% and 4.85%), positive EPS from FY26 and free cash flow positive from FY27.

Three judgements matter most for the next Analyst:

  1. The 2026 margin step overstates the lever. About 90bp of it was a reallocation of acquisition spending (partly into gross margin as Rx bonuses) that cannot recur at that size, and Q2-Q3 are seasonal peaks. The margin therefore rises about 40-65bp a year from here, reaching 5% around FY30 rather than FY28. Consensus is about right for FY27 EBITDA and slightly high for FY28 margin, but slightly low on revenue.
  2. The 18-month cone is set by management choices more than by demand. Acquisition spend around the 2027 co-payment rise is worth three times a typical revenue miss, and the new chief executive's first plan (12 November) can re-weight it. Sevenum commissioning is the operational risk, with a 2021 precedent.
  3. Below EBITDA, definitions dominate. PPA, non-cash convertible interest, tax and stock options each move FY27 EPS by €0.15-0.70; the visible broker range is mostly definitional. The deferred tax asset recognition and the 2030 convertible put are the two capital-structure events inside the horizon.

Net: positive for the investment case on direction and predictability, neutral on the size of near-term upgrades to EBITDA, with the upside lying in revenue and in below-EBITDA clarity rather than in the margin.

P-08

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

D565th of 9

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: Below operating profit, the next four years will look nothing like the last six. Between FY19 and FY25 the items below EBIT turned small operating losses into large net losses (net income -€12m to -€78m every year) and the share count rose 49% (13.5m to 20.1m) through capital raises and convertibles. From FY26 the below-EBIT items become modest and predictable: net finance cost of €13-17m (only about €5m of it cash), a low-but-rising tax rate, a near-zero minority charge, no buybacks, no dividends and share count growth of about 0.5% a year. The consequence is that EPS will grow far faster than EBITDA (model basis €0.82 in FY26 to €5.72 in FY30), while free cash flow converts only 40-80% of net income because of leases, working capital and the gap between capex and D&A. The most important point for the next Analyst is that the visible spread of broker EPS is mainly definitional, so "consensus EPS" is not a reliable yardstick for FY26-FY27.

The items, one by one (my forecasts):

Item FY19-FY25 history FY26 FY27 FY28 FY29 FY30 Note
Net financial items (€m) -8 to -20 a year -22 -17 -16 -15 -13 About €9-10m a year is non-cash convertible accretion (P-02)
Tax rate on model-basis pre-tax profit 3% to 19%, meaningless on losses; 41.2% in H1 2026 22% 18% 18% 26% 26% See the tax paragraph below
Cash tax rate near zero about €4m about 15% about 15% about 15% about 18% Dutch 50% loss-offset rule
Minorities (€m) MediService consolidated loss of €3.5m in FY25 (so minorities added back) -0.5 -1 -1.5 -2 -2 Galenica's 49% of a business that earns almost nothing after PPA
