Buybacks and dividends none none none none none none Policy, Dutch legal reserves and the 2030 put
PPA amortisation (excluded from the model's D&A, €m) -13 -13 -12 -11 -10 My estimate; not disclosed

Tax: the mechanism the Consumer must get right. Redcare's statutory rates are 25.8% (Netherlands, where most profit is earned) and 32% (Germany). It had €311.9m of unrecognised Dutch tax losses at end-2025 (€345.9m in total). Dutch law lets losses offset taxable profit fully only up to €1m and 50% above that, so even with large losses the Dutch cash tax rate is about 13% of profit, and the group's cash rate about 15-18% once German and Swiss entities and non-deductible items are added. Losses on this scale last into the early 2030s. The accounting rate differs from the cash rate in three phases: (1) FY26-FY28: no deferred tax asset is recognised, so the P&L charge is roughly the cash charge plus noise (18-22%); (2) the year of recognition, which I assume is FY28 year-end once two profitable years make utilisation "probable" under IAS 12: a one-off credit of roughly €60-70m (about €3 a share) in reported earnings, which brokers' adjusted EPS will exclude; (3) thereafter a P&L rate near the statutory blend (about 26%) while cash tax stays near 15-18%. So adjusted EPS growth in FY29 is clipped by about eight points of tax rate that has no cash effect, and free cash flow exceeds the P&L would suggest by about €14-20m a year from FY29.

Why broker EPS for FY27 ranges from €0.84 (Jefferies) to €2.04 (Deutsche Bank) on near-identical EBITDA (€137-142m). I disagree with the AlphaSense report that the non-cash convertible interest alone explains it. Each of four items is worth a similar amount per share:

Item Range across plausible treatments Effect on FY27 EPS
Including or excluding PPA amortisation (about €13m) in or out about €0.50
Tax rate (about 0% "loss shelter" to about 25%) on €45-57m of pre-tax profit 0-25% about €0.55-0.70
Net finance cost (mwb models €9m a year; IFRS gives about €17m) €9-17m about €0.35
Stock option cost (about €3m) in or out about €0.15

Jefferies and mwb publish reported EPS; Deutsche Bank's figure looks closer to an adjusted basis with a low tax charge. The FactSet median of €1.32 is therefore a blend of bases. On a like-for-like reported basis my FY27 EPS is €1.57 (19% above the median) and my FY28 is €2.85 (in line). On the model's basis (excluding PPA and stock options) my FY27 is €2.20 and FY28 €3.46.

Net income. Reported-like net income of about €4-5m in FY26 (H1 was -€5.8m; H2 about +€10m), €32m in FY27 (the first full year of positive net income in the company's 25-year history), €59m in FY28 (plus the one-off deferred tax credit if recognised), €80m in FY29 and €110m in FY30.

Free cash flow and conversion (after capex, leases, cash interest, cash tax and working capital, before minority dividends):

€m FY26 FY27 FY28 FY29 FY30

Cash one-offs -4 -3 -3 -3 -3

Conversion is lower than the first set's 0.9-1.1x because leases (about €12m) and working capital growth (about €20m) are real cash costs that the P&L does not show, and capex (1.8% of revenue) roughly equals D&A excluding PPA. FactSet's consensus free cash flow (-€35m, €28m, €64m, €151m) appears to be before lease payments; on that basis my figures are -€14m, €30m, €57m and €85m, so I am in line for FY26-FY27 and below for FY28-FY29, the gap being my lower FY28-FY29 EBITDA.

Share issuance and buybacks. The company has ruled out buybacks until "consistent structural net profitability", Dutch law blocks distributions while capitalised development sits in restricted reserves (€25.6m at end-2025), and the reported 2030 put on the convertible will absorb most of the cash built in FY27-FY29. I therefore forecast none through FY30. Equity issuance for cash is unnecessary on any of my scenarios. The share count rises only through option exercises (about 0.1m a year), plus 1.73m shares if the convertible converts (not in my base; it requires a share price above €173.74).

Margins/growth vs. past

6 answers

C-53How high are gross and operating profit margins, and how are they trending?F5010th of 11full answer under 4c Financial profile
C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

A741st of 11

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: The scope is large and comes from a few big areas, supported by several small ones. Peer precedent (Apotea; DocMorris' retail media; Galenica's services mix) shows it is achievable. It is fully congruent with the stated strategy.

Big areas (my estimate of mid-term EBITDA potential, illustrative):

  1. German Rx volume through the fixed-cost network. The single largest. Each additional €100m of German Rx revenue (about 1.4-2m packs) plausibly adds €10-15m of EBITDA once Sevenum is commissioned.
  2. Fixum. Net €9.5-13.3m (2027) rising to €12.3-17.2m (2028) and €20.8-29.1m (2030) on Deutsche Bank's two scenarios.
  3. Marketing ratio at about 5%. Already largely realised in 2026. Further leverage comes from cohort layering.
  4. Retail media and marketplace. Under 5% of sales today, with high incremental margins (DocMorris earns mid-double-digit EBITDA margins on retail media). Raising this to, say, 1-2% of revenue at high margins could add 50-100bp to the group margin. This is the lever the new marketplace chief executive and finance director were hired for.

Small areas:

  • International moving from 0.9% towards DACH margins: €12-18m potential.
  • Own brands.
  • AI pricing.
  • The Swiss assortment.
  • B2B wholesale.
  • Swiss liberalisation from 2029-30 and EU cross-border prescriptions from 2029, both options.

Peer precedents:

  • Apotea: gross margin held at 27.5% while Rx mix rose; EBIT margin heading above 5% by 2027 on automation at Varberg.
  • DocMorris: retail media and TeleClinic margins.
  • Galenica: services mix (Products and Care margin up 120bp to 47.5%).

Congruence. These are exactly the stated priorities: Rx focus, operating leverage, marketplace and retail media, own brands, AI and EPS focus.

Conclusion. Reaching the 5% EBITDA target by FY28 is plausible on mechanical levers 1-3 alone. Reaching 6-7% by FY30 needs lever 4. The long-term above-8% ambition needs Apotea-like maturity, and is not evidenced yet.

C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

C-585th of 11

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: The headline has decelerated. The core engine has not.

Group growth.

  • Ran at 25-28% from Q4 2024 to Q3 2025 (FY24 +31.8%, organic +25.6%; FY25 +24.1%).
  • Fell to 17.8% in Q4 2025, then 18.4% (Q1), 20.3% (Q2) and about 20% (Q3 2026).
  • So it is running 5-8 points below its own three-year norm.
  • FY26 guidance implies about 13% in H2 and 8-15% in Q4.

The cause is the non-Rx half, not the core:

  • DACH non-Rx growth slowed to 9% in Q1 2026, recovered to 13% in Q2, dipped to single digits in July and rebounded in August and September.
  • The online OTC market is about 25% penetrated, competitive and weather-exposed.
  • Swiss Rx grows at a steady 15-17%.

The core engine (German Rx) has accelerated:

  • From more than €500m in FY25 to €730-760m guided for FY26.
  • Quarterly growth of 55%, 58% and 56%.

The input change force (the e-Rx online shift) is not slowing in value terms:

  • BEVH online medicines +13.9% in Q2;
  • DocMorris Rx +78% in Q3;
  • gematik volumes stable at 48-52m a month, with the usual August dip (42.9m).

Two leading indicators of the input force are softening:

  1. Active German Rx customer additions: about +60-70 thousand in Q2 2026, a slower sequential pace.
  2. App downloads down 18% year on year in September.

Growth is shifting from new patients to more revenue per patient (basket +14%, frequency, six-month cycles). The Risks report's "false friend" hypothesis lives exactly here: growth in revenue per patient can mask stalling acquisition.

Judgement. The deceleration is real at group level and explained by OTC and by lapping. The engine is intact. The risk is that German Rx growth halves in 2027 if acquisition does not re-accelerate around PoPP and the co-payment rise. Even then it would run at 20-30%, still above consensus.

On revenue. My rough segment build (non-Rx +8-10%, Swiss Rx +10-15%) implies consensus has German Rx growing only about 15-20% a year from about €745m to roughly €1.0-1.05bn in FY28. That is much less than the run-rate (+56% in Q3), less than the brokers' own penetration narrative (low-to-mid single digits by FY28 with a 67% share would give about €1.5bn) and less than Deutsche Bank's own Fixum package model (17.6m packs at €50 = €880m in 2027).

Credibility. Given the change forces (accelerating e-Rx shift, six-month cycles, co-payments, CardLink certainty) and the foundations (67% share, fixed-cost network, 90% repeat), the revenue gap is not credible to the downside. German Rx in FY27 is more likely +25-35% than +15-20%. Group growth is more likely 14-16% than 12.7%.

Margin. The margin path is credible: it needs only the committed levers. Upside comes from retail media. Downside comes from co-payment competition.

EPS. Most at risk from below-EBITDA items, in both directions.

Share count +49% FY19-FY25 +2% FY26-FY30

Concentration of the drivers (useful for the third set's question D-03). Of the FY25-FY30 revenue increase of about €2.38bn, German Rx contributes 46%, DACH non-Rx 24%, International 17% and Swiss Rx 14%; revenue drivers are therefore moderately diffuse. Of the FY26-FY30 EBITDA increase of about €157m, roughly half comes from volume through the fixed-cost network (German Rx alone about a third), roughly 40% from four discrete levers (Sevenum, the marketing-plus-bonus ratio, retail media, the 2027 fee step), offset by about 30% of cost inflation, co-payment cover and new-management investment. The margin outcome hinges on two management decisions (acquisition spend around the 2027 co-payment rise; how much of the gain the new chief executive reinvests) and one project (Sevenum). Regulation is the third, external, swing factor: the fee schedule and the legality of bonuses.

Where the net impact differs from consensus. Revenue: +1% (FY27) to +2% (FY28) above. EBITDA: in line in FY27, about 3% below in FY28 and about 15% below the two-broker FY29 figure. EPS: above on a like-for-like reported basis in FY27, in line in FY28. Free cash flow: in line in FY26-FY27, below in FY28-FY29. So the case for upgrades over the next 18 months rests on revenue and on below-EBITDA clarity, not on margin.

Forecasts and upgrades

8

Probably upgrades this financial year to

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

The fourth set as a whole

–50

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Our assessment is, for the next 18 months, largely reflected already.

  • Guidance is the most favourable of the three. It is set conservatively by a finance director who has built a record of doing so, and raises to revenue and German Rx are more likely than not in both FY26 and FY27. But the raises will be of revenue; the margin, which drives EPS, will be met, not raised; and the first FY27 margin range is likely to sit with consensus at its top.
  • Sell-side estimates equal our forecasts at EBITDA. The upgrades that followed the March 2026 reset have brought consensus to where our work independently arrives, and the breadth of revisions, strongly positive to late October, should fade to neutral. The EPS gap in our favour is real only to the extent that our tax rate is right.
  • The narrative contains the positives in full and the cautions in part.

What this means for the memo's questions on expectations.

  1. There is no large unrecognised positive to point to over the next six months. That period is more likely to bring fading revision momentum, a soft non-Rx fourth quarter and a cautious first guide than a surprise to the upside. The most useful event in it is not Redcare's: it is what DocMorris says about co-payment cover on 12 November.
  2. The favourable gap, where there is one, opens in months 6 to 18. If Q1 2027 shows a clean Sevenum go-live and contained co-payment cover, the path is: a first half that is clearly profitable on the reported basis, a probable revenue raise in June or July, the fee gain reported in full, and then H2 2027 proving that the margin rises without help. Each step is expected by analysts; what is not yet granted by investors is that they will be delivered. The return on offer is the return to delivering what is already forecast, from a valuation that discounts doubt, not the return to beating forecasts.
  3. Beyond 18 months the gap is slightly against us. The company's 5% target and the higher broker estimates for FY28 and FY29 sit above our forecasts.
  4. The honest description of momentum for the memo is: guidance momentum positive and likely to stay so on revenue; estimate momentum at or near its peak; narrative momentum positive among analysts and unproven among investors.

The earlier sets ended on the thought that the case needed a model materially above consensus to be attractive. The work since then has not produced one. It has produced a different and narrower proposition: a business whose direction is unusually well founded, forecast in line with consensus, priced with more doubt than the forecasts contain, and about to pass through the five months in which that doubt is either confirmed or removed.

I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

–60

Layer 4 · Guidance

Judgement: The most likely pattern over the next 18 months is the 2026 pattern repeated: guidance for revenue and German Rx raised, guidance for the margin met in its upper half and not raised. The first FY27 guidance, on 3 March 2027, will probably be set with its margin midpoint a little below today's consensus, which is where this finance director placed FY26. A cut to margin guidance during 2027 is a minority outcome, about one chance in six, with two identifiable causes. Beyond 18 months, the target of a 5% margin "within three years" is more likely to be reached late than on time, and every broker in FactSet is already below it for FY28. All probabilities below are my judgement.

The events. A trading update on 11 January 2027; FY26 results and the first FY27 guidance on 3 March 2027; Q1 results in early May 2027; half-year results in late July 2027; Q3 results in late October 2027; a trading update in January 2028; FY27 results and the first FY28 guidance in early March 2028; Q1 2028 in early May 2028. In 2026 both raises came as ad hoc releases, on 15 June and 28 September, ahead of the quarterly reports. Redcare has no strategy day scheduled (reading notes).

FY26 guidance: two events left (11 January and 3 March 2027).

Element Guidance Where I expect the outcome Raised or beaten Met Reduced or missed
Revenue growth 16-18% about 17.7% 15% 83% 2%
German Rx revenue €730-760m about €760m 45% 53% 2%
Non-Rx growth 10-12% about 10% to 10.5% none 65% 35%
Adjusted EBITDA margin 2.5-3.0% about 2.9% 15% (above 3.0%) 82% (70% in the upper half) 3%
  • What drives a German Rx raise. Nine-month revenue is €545m, so the range implies €185-215m for Q4. Q3 was €197m and each quarter of 2026 has added €12-17m to the one before. Jefferies expects another raise before the year-end.
  • What drives a non-Rx shortfall. Non-Rx grew about 11-12% in the first half and 9.4% in Q3, and German non-Rx began October below 10%. The outcome sits on the bottom edge of the range. This is the one element of FY26 guidance at risk, and the Consumer should expect it to be trimmed or narrowly missed without consequence for the group range, because German Rx fills the gap.
  • What drives the margin. The nine-month margin is 2.9%. Guidance allows Q4 anywhere from 1.4% to 3.4%; the company wants freedom to spend in Q4, and Q4 margins were −0.7% in 2024 and 1.6% in 2025. I expect about 3.0%.

FY27 guidance: set on 3 March 2027, then tested at four events.

How it will be set. In March 2026 the same finance director guided revenue growth at 13-15% (the outcome will be about 17.7%), German Rx at "more than €670m" (about €760m) and the margin at 2.5-3.0% (about 2.9%). Revenue was set about 3.5 points below the outcome; the top of the margin range was set at the outcome. Applied to our FY27 forecast, that points to revenue growth guided in the low double digits (Deutsche Bank expects the same), German Rx at roughly €850-900m or more, and a margin range of about 3.0-3.5%.

The first FY27 margin range, against consensus of 3.5% Probability
Midpoint below 3.5% (consensus at or near the top of the range) 55%
Midpoint at about 3.5% 35%
Midpoint above 3.5% 10%

3 March 2027 is therefore the date on which an adverse share-price reaction is most likely without any change in the fundamentals. The March 2026 precedent was severe because expectations were far above the guide; this time consensus is modest and the pattern is understood, so I would expect a far smaller effect.

How it will move during 2027.

Raised at least once Met without change Reduced
Revenue and German Rx 55% 35% 10%
Adjusted EBITDA margin 20% 63% 17%
  • A revenue raise needs only the present pace in German Rx, and the co-payment rise adds a reason for patients to move online. The likely moment is June or July 2027, as in 2026.
  • A margin raise is unlikely before the Q3 report, for three reasons. This finance director has never raised a margin range. Q1 is the weakest quarter and carries the Sevenum go-live. And the fee gives its help in the first half only (D-03), so H2 2027 must be seen before management will bank it. If the margin range is raised, it will be because Sevenum delivers management's 50bp and the co-payment contest stays contained.
  • A margin cut has two causes. The first is a co-payment contest that goes beyond targeted cover; each €2.50 covered on a quarter of packs costs about €10m, or 25bp. The second is a lost quarter at Sevenum, worth €3-8m. A weak non-Rx season alone would be absorbed inside the range, as in 2026. A ban on co-payment waivers would be a cut of a different order, and I put it at 5% or less within the window.
  • The free cash flow target (positive in FY27) is new and is the one target without a cushion. I put the chance of meeting it at about 60-65% (D-02).

FY28 guidance (March 2028) and the mid-term target. The first FY28 margin range will be set weeks before the CardLink licence ends and, on our forecast, around 4%. A range of that kind would concede, without saying so, that 5% is not an FY28 figure.

The 5% margin target "within three years" (set March 2026) Probability
Raised 5%
Met on time (an FY28 margin of about 4.8% or more) 15%
Kept in words but reached late, or quietly re-dated 65%
Formally lowered 15%

The ambition of a margin above 8% in the long term will be left alone; it is not evidenced and nobody relies on it. One further change is likely and helpful: a move to guidance on EPS and free cash flow, which the finance director announced in March 2026. It would oblige the company to guide the items below EBITDA for the first time.

How our assessment compares with what guidance reflects. For the next 18 months, guidance is set below our forecast on revenue and German Rx and at our forecast on the margin. That is favourable in a modest and familiar way: the raises are probable but they will be raises of revenue. For the period beyond, the company's target is above our forecast (4.0% in FY28), which is unfavourable, though the sell-side has already discounted it.

Layer 4 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: Consensus has caught up with our assessment. After upgrades of 14% to FY27 EBITDA and 85% to FY27 EPS in six months, consensus FY27 EBITDA (€138m) equals our base case (€139m) and our probability-weighted figure (€137m). Over the next 18 months I expect revenue forecasts to be nudged up, FY27 EBITDA forecasts to stay within about 3% of today's level with the first move down, and the breadth of revisions to fall from strongly positive to about neutral within three months. Beyond 18 months, consensus margins for FY28 and the two-broker figure for FY29 are more likely to be cut than raised. The one gap in our favour is in EPS, and most of it is a tax assumption, not operations.

Where consensus stands against our forecasts (after my input changes).

FY26 FY27 FY28
Revenue: consensus; ours €3,456m; €3,461m (+0.2%) €3,894m; €3,925m (+0.8%) €4,313m; €4,377m (+1.5%)
Adjusted EBITDA: consensus; ours €99m; €100m (+1%) €138m; €139m (+1%) €182m; €175m (−4%)
EPS: consensus (a blend of bases); ours, reported-like −€0.21; about −€0.1 €1.32; €1.55 (+17%) €2.85; €2.85

Three cautions on this table.

  1. The model summary shows our EPS 63% above consensus for FY27. That is a difference of basis and must not be quoted as a gap in expectations. The model's EPS excludes the amortisation of acquired intangibles and the stock option cost; most brokers' figures include them.
  2. Most of the genuine 17% is tax. We charge 18% in FY27 and FY28, because Dutch losses shelter half of profit above €1m; Deutsche Bank and mwb charge about 25%. That is worth about €0.15 to €0.19 a share. The annual report documents the rule and the losses, but the company has not guided a tax rate, so this gap is ours to lose as well as to win.
  3. FY26 will be a loss on the reported basis. Reported net income for the nine months is about −€5m, and Q4 should add only a small profit. Consensus (−€0.21) is about right. The second set's reported-like +€0.22 is too high, and the model's +€0.75 is on the adjusted basis. The first profitable year the market will recognise is FY27.

Why the breadth of revisions will fade.

  1. Arithmetic. FactSet's revision figures for FY27 EPS are +16% over one month, +35% over three and +85% over six. The large upgrades came between April and September; as they leave the windows the figures fall even if nobody cuts.
  2. The first changes after Q3 were down. Deutsche Bank's FY27 EBITDA went from €138m to €134m and its EPS from €2.04 to €1.98; mwb edged down; Jefferies did not move. I infer this by comparing FactSet at 12 October with notes dated 29 and 30 October.
  3. The fourth quarter gives no reason to raise margins. October non-Rx was soft, International is back in loss, and management has kept its room to spend.
  4. The FY27 guide is likely to sit below consensus on the margin (I-01).
  5. Stale estimates will refresh in both directions. Two brokers last updated on 30 July: one has FY27 EBITDA of €125m, the other €153m. On revenue the refresh is upward (mwb at €3,749m and UBS at €3,763m for FY27 are well below the rest), which is why revenue breadth should stay positive.

The dashboard rated sell-side earnings momentum A on 23 October, the best in its sector. I expect that reading to weaken by early 2027. The memo should treat it as a description of the last six months, not of the next six.

For EPS the direction is less predictable than for EBITDA, because the largest changes will be definitional: as the company starts to guide below EBITDA, brokers will converge on a treatment of the convertible's interest, the amortisation and tax, and the median can move by 10-20% with no change in the business.

Secondarily, 18 months to three years. FY28 consensus EBITDA is €182m, a 4.2% margin, with brokers from €155m to €200m (3.7% to 4.6%). Ours is €175m (4.0%). I put a cut at 45%, no change at 35% and an upgrade at 20%. An upgrade would need retail media to scale or an overhead programme to be announced; neither is in evidence. The FY29 figure of €254m rests on two brokers and a 5.2% margin; ours is €215m. It is not a consensus and should not be used as one.

How our assessment compares with what estimates reflect. For the next 18 months the gap is close to nil at EBITDA, slightly positive on revenue, and positive on EPS for a reason that is uncertain. Beyond 18 months it is slightly negative. The cycle of upgrades that followed the March 2026 reset has done its work.

11d

Current multiple

–not yet ratedMemo topic 11, : the Rater's score1 answer
D-0758
D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

–58

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: The shares move almost entirely on their own. The dashboard's peer basket explains about 4% of Redcare's daily moves over the last six months and never more than 12% in two years, so the multiple is company-driven. There are two qualifications: the one true peer, DocMorris, is not in the basket, and the market regime reaches the stock through its style (a loss-making, convertible-financed share that has risen fast), not through its sector. For the case this cuts both ways. The return will depend on Redcare's own delivery, which is what this work analyses; and there is no sector to cushion a stumble.

The evidence (rolling windows of 126 trading days; basket of six sector peers, equal-weighted).

Measure Latest window (15 May to 6 November 2026) Range over 24 month-ends
Correlation with the basket 0.19 0.11 to 0.34
Beta to the basket 0.77 0.39 to 1.07
Share of daily variance explained by the basket 4% 1% to 12%
Idiosyncratic share 96% 88% to 99%
Six-month return +51.9%, against +13.2% for the basket

The closest single peer is Apotea, the Swedish online pharmacy, at a correlation of 0.21, which explains 4%. Galenica, Redcare's partner and a 10.4% shareholder, explains 1%.

How I read it.

  1. The sector label is not an economic grouping for this stock. The basket holds hearing-aid, optical and medical-distribution companies. Low co-movement with them says little beyond that.
  2. There is a two-stock factor that the basket misses. Redcare and DocMorris answer to the same monthly gematik and app data, the same fee schedule and the same politics. I have no price series for DocMorris in my files, so I cannot measure it, but the first set's record of price moves on DocMorris news supports it.
  3. Within that pair, the relative multiple has moved for company reasons. DocMorris' shares had risen 137% in 2026 by mid-October against 11% for Redcare (Jefferies), and Redcare trades at about 0.45 times FY27 enterprise value to sales against 0.59 to 0.65 times for DocMorris, despite earning the higher margin. The market has paid for the rate of change (a rival reaching break-even) over the level. That tells the Consumer what Redcare's multiple responds to: evidence that its own margin is rising, quarter by quarter.
  4. Every large move in the last two years was a company or theme event. A 25% fall on dm's entry (December 2024), the fall to about €30 on the March 2026 reset, 15% down on the chief executive's departure, about 10% up on the September guidance raise, and a fall on the Q3 remark about October non-Rx sales.

What this means for the multiple.

  • The dashboard shows a 24-month-forward P/E of 30.1 times, at the 13th percentile of the stock's ten-year history. That history is not a usable yardstick: earnings were at or near zero for most of it, and the five-year range runs from 21 times to 6,774 times. Sales and EBITDA multiples (about 13 times FY27 EBITDA) and the profitable peers (Apotea at 23 times earnings, Galenica at 20 times) are better guides.
  • The model summary's return table should not be presented as an expectation. It applies a guideline fair multiple of 114.81 times, which comes from the same distorted history, to FY29 EPS and so reaches a value of €491 a share and a return of +531% by the end of 2027. Its split of that return (−742% from the multiple, −198% from earnings) is arithmetically unstable because the starting earnings are negative. The fair multiple is Viewforth's to set; my view, for the Consumer's use, is that this guideline cannot be applied to a company at the point where its earnings turn positive.
  • A sober cross-check: at the end of 2027 the two-year-forward EPS is the FY29 figure, about €4.3 on the model basis. At 20 to 30 times, which brackets the profitable peers and Redcare's own multiple today, that is about €86 to €128, a return of about +10% to +65% over 14 months. On that view the whole return comes from earnings arriving, with the multiple flat or lower, and it is exposed in full to the EPS cone of D-04.

The link to predictability. Because the multiple follows Redcare's own news, the forecasts in this work carry directly into the share price: there is little sector noise to dilute a correct call and none to hide a wrong one. The style exposure the first set described (C-07, C-82) is the one outside force that could move the multiple against good results.

Outside the eleven ratings

Answers not placed under any of the eleven ratings

The table the ratings map uses places these nowhere; each is shown with the reason the table records.

Why it is not placed It asks whether the stock fits the sector's historical patterns of share price and financial outperformance, a cross-check across many topics rather than one of the eleven.

C-84

What do historical precedents, rules of thumb and the empirical evidence show about how companies in this sector and industry have typically generated share price, financial and operational outperformance, and underperformance, and to what extent does this company fit those patterns?

A701st of 11

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

Judgement: The pattern in online pharmacy and regulated-price retail is clear. Outperformance comes when channel-shift volume runs through fixed-cost infrastructure while marketing intensity falls, and the shares re-rate as profit and free cash flow appear. Underperformance comes from bought growth (promotions, price wars), sub-scale cash burn ending in dilutive raises, logistics failures and regulatory shocks. Redcare currently fits the outperformance pattern. It has, in its own history, also fitted every element of the underperformance pattern.

Precedents of outperformance:

  • Apotea (Sweden). Rode deregulation and e-Rx to 27% online penetration. It is the "gold standard" (DNB Carnegie): automated Varberg hub, gross margin stable at 27.5% despite a higher Rx mix, EBIT margin 4-6% heading above 5% by 2027, target 7-8%. Adjusted operating profit is projected to grow 26% a year in 2026-28. It trades at 23x P/E.
  • Redcare 2026. The shares are up 62.6% over six months (from about €30 to €77.90): German Rx up 58%, marketing down 210bp, a 10-year high margin. The sector digest notes that "where regulation sets the price, gains come from volume and fixed costs".
  • Galenica. Steady omnichannel compounding at an 8-9% EBITDA margin. It is the only B.8 stock up over 12 months, the defensive version of the same pool.

Precedents of underperformance:

  • Zur Rose / DocMorris. About 98% of value destroyed. Repeated capital raises (CHF 200m), forced disposals (the Swiss business to Medbase), short interest up to 32.6%, activist campaigns. All from sub-scale cash burn and slower e-Rx adoption than promised.
  • Redcare's own history:
  • 2018: profit forecast cut on German price competition.
  • 2021: Q2 growth slowed to 7.3%, the Sevenum warehouse migration caused a sales-outlook cut, Q3 sales missed by about 9%, and an Rx bonus ban landed.
  • 2025: market capitalisation fell 52%; the dm entry scare took 25% off the shares.
  • March 2026: a margin reset took the shares to about €30.
  • CVS (vets). Shows that consolidator returns can coexist with a weak share price when organic volume stalls and margins are "constructed" (credits, mix). The analogue risk for Redcare is a "constructed" margin from regulatory fees and promotional timing.

Rules of thumb that apply:

  1. In a fixed-price market, own volume and the cost per order. Redcare does both.
  2. E-retailers re-rate when free cash flow turns positive. Redcare's turn is in FY27 by consensus.
  3. Growth bought with incentives cannot fund margin. Test it by whether the margin rises with growth; it did in Q2 and Q3 2026.
  4. Logistics migrations cause stumbles. Sevenum commissions in Q1 2027.
  5. Regulated moats are absolute and fragile.
  6. A long uptrend banks its upside. The stock is up 62.6% in six months, but is "proven-but-young": its 200-day average has been rising in only one of the last 12 months, and the Stage 2 bucket is B_early.

Fit. Strong fit to the outperformance pattern for the next 12-18 months: volume accelerating, cost per order falling, marketing falling, free cash flow inflecting. Partial fit to the risk pattern: a new chief executive, a logistics transition, co-payment competition and political noise. Redcare's own record says the market punishes it severely on any stumble.

Why it is not placed The table holds this question in reserve for Riskiness sub-items that are not yet built, so it is not yet placed.

C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

F3010th of 11

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: On consensus earnings at a trough multiple, the shares could fall about 30-45% (to roughly €43-55). In a fundamental bear case (stalled penetration, margin stuck near 3-4%), about 55-65% (to roughly €27-35). The latter is where the stock actually traded in March 2026.

Bridge. EV €1,855m; net debt plus minorities less associates about €252m (FactSet); 20.6m diluted shares; price €77.90.

Jefferies' severe scenarios: €25 (below 5% penetration through FY28, 4% EBITDA margin, 9.4x EV/EBITDA) and €35 (9.8x adjusted) €25-35 -55% to -68%
Jefferies' February "what would change my mind" case: penetration below 5% by FY28, margin capped at 4.0% €50 -36%

The 52-week low was €30.20 (March 2026). Ten-year 24-month forward P/E bands: a "crash" band at 27.1x (10th percentile). P/E is not a usable trough anchor at the earnings inflection (EPS near zero in FY26).

Fair trough. About €45-55, or -30% to -42%. This is the price a sceptical market would pay for consensus earnings without crediting the growth runway: roughly 9-10x FY27 EV/EBITDA, about 0.35x EV/sales. A fundamental break, a bonus ban or a stall in penetration, would take it to about €30-35.

Peer cross-check:

  • DocMorris trades at 0.57-0.59x FY27 EV/sales against Redcare's 0.45-0.48x. So Redcare's current multiple is already below the peer's, which limits multiple downside relative to the sector.
  • CVS sits at about 8x EBITDA as a regulation-overhung consolidator. That is the "regulatory overhang" multiple Redcare would get if German policy turned hostile.

Upside-to-downside. Consensus target median €96 (+23%) against a fair trough of -30% to -42%. The asymmetry is not compelling on consensus alone. It becomes attractive only if the Consumer's model sits materially above consensus for FY27-28, which my answers suggest is plausible on German Rx.

Why it is not placed It rates the staircase of news the market can look forward to, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or the catalysts as such.

Group judgement

Stock market narrative

A721st of 11

Layer 1 · Stock market narrative

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The forward narrative has a clear, dated staircase:

  • Next six months: the new chief executive's CMD; the Fixum and co-payment changes in January; the first FY27 guidance in March; Sevenum commissioning.
  • Months 6-18: the first positive EPS and free cash flow year; visible automation savings; PoPP Phase 2.
  • Months 18-36: the 5% margin target year, possible shareholder returns, and long-dated EU and Swiss options.

The narrative can move from "loss-making e-retailer" to "regulated-volume compounder".

Its weaknesses:

  • dependence on a three-week-old chief executive's first message;
  • a perception-fragile, momentum-extended share price in a risk-off regime;
  • a 2028 regulatory transition (CardLink to PoPP) that turns from a tailwind into a test.

Why it is not placed It rates how well the earlier answers agree with each other, which is a check on the analysis rather than on the company.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

–64

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: The two earlier sets agree on what drives the company and on where the risks sit. They disagree on how fast the margin rises. The Q3 results and the later research settle most of that disagreement in the second set's favour, although partly for different reasons from the ones the second set gave. Congruence is therefore good on structure and direction and moderate on magnitude, and three factual anchors that both sets shared have since moved. Nothing I have found contradicts the spine of the case. The one implication that jars is that, on the first set's own test, this is no longer a case that rests on estimates being too low.

Where the sets support each other (the load-bearing agreements).

  1. The profit engine. The first set's central judgement, that German Rx profit is earned per pack at a state-set fee, is the foundation of the second set's model (about 16m packs in FY27 at about €8.8 of gross profit a pack). The two fit without strain.
  2. Non-Rx is the cyclical half and the main source of revenue error. Q3 confirmed it: group growth fell from 20.3% to 17.7% in one quarter while German Rx held at +56%.
  3. The risk map. Both sets name the same four sources: the state, the behaviour of the duopoly around the 2027 co-payment rise, the Sevenum commissioning, and a perception-fragile share price. The second set attaches numbers and probabilities to the first set's list; it does not redraw it.
  4. How the company guides. Revenue guidance is a floor; margin guidance is never raised. Q3 behaved exactly so: guidance was left unchanged with the nine-month margin already at 2.9%.
  5. Capital. No returns, no acquisitions, capital spending below 2% of revenue. The convertible's put is now confirmed: 16 April 2030, at 106.93% of par, about €321m. The company's own annual report says a refinancing risk could arise in 2030 depending on the share price.

Where they diverge, and my ruling.

(a) The speed of margin improvement. The first set expected incremental margins of 8-10%, an FY27 margin of 3.7-4.0%, 4.5-5% by FY28 and EBITDA above consensus. The second set expects 40-65bp a year: 3.55% in FY27, 4.0% in FY28 and 5% around FY30. I rule for the second set's numbers, but its explanation needs correcting. It argued that about 120bp of the 170bp fall in the Q2 gross margin was "bonuses, retail media and other", so that most of the marketing saving had been re-routed into gross margin. The company's own bridge, which the second set did not have, attributes the fall to Rx mix and to non-Rx price competition. The table sets out both quarters on that basis, in basis points of group revenue, year on year. The disclosed items are the company's; the allocation is mine.

Q2 2026 Q3 2026
Adjusted EBITDA margin +100 (2.5% to 3.5%) +100 (2.4% to 3.4%)
Gross margin −170 −30
of which German Rx mix about −90 (company: −110bp of the DACH margin) about −90 (my estimate; Rx growth was the same)
of which non-Rx pricing and margin about −65 (company: −80bp of the DACH margin) about +65 (German non-Rx gross margin +160bp)
of which the July 2026 fee step none about +25 (€0.65 on about 3.4m packs, €2.2m)
of which International, retail media and other about −15 about −30
Marketing ratio +210 +100
of which an absolute cut in spending about +90 (about €51m to €43.6m) none (about €42.4m to €41.5m)
of which revenue growing over flat spending about +120 about +100
All other operating costs +60 +30

Four things follow.

  1. Bonuses were a small part of the gross margin fall, not the larger part. Rx bonuses are deducted from revenue under IFRS 15 (now confirmed), so they sit inside the Rx figures, but mix and non-Rx pricing explain about 155 of the 170bp. The second set underestimated mix because it used a ten-point gap between the Rx and non-Rx gross margins. The per-pack arithmetic that both sets rely on gives a German Rx gross margin of about 13-14% of revenue (about €7.9 of gross profit on about €58 of revenue a pack in H1 2026), not the 18-22% the first set quoted from Deutsche Bank. The gap to non-Rx is therefore 16-17 points, and each point of revenue that moves to German Rx takes about 17bp off the group gross margin, not 8-12bp.
  2. The marketing saving was half a one-off cut and half arithmetic. About 90bp of Q2's 210bp was a cut in absolute spending, which is finished: in Q3 spending was flat and the whole 100bp came from revenue growing over it. Much of that arithmetic gain is the mirror image of Rx mix, because Rx revenue needs little marketing and less fulfilment per euro. The first set therefore double-counted when it read the marketing ratio as leverage and the gross margin as a separate, harmless mix effect.
  3. The repeatable gain is about 40-90bp a year at 18-20% revenue growth. Removing the mix effect and its mirror image, the one-off cut, the fee step and the swing in non-Rx margin leaves about 90bp in Q2 and about 40bp in Q3. At the 13% growth forecast for FY27 that is about 40-45bp. Adding the fee (about 17bp) and Sevenum (about 20bp) and deducting co-payment cover (about 15bp) gives 3.5-3.6% for FY27. The second set's 3.55% survives a corrected decomposition; the first set's 3.7-4.0% does not.
  4. The largest moving part in either quarter is the non-Rx gross margin, which swung from about −65bp to about +65bp of group revenue. That is a pricing choice in the competitive half of the business, and it belongs with marketing and bonuses in one cluster that I call the commercial give-back (D-03).

(b) Seasonality. The second set is right that Q2 and Q3 are peak quarters. Q3 came in at 3.4%, not at a rising run-rate.

(c) What consensus implies for German Rx. The second set is right that consensus revenue already implies about 25% growth in German Rx and that non-Rx is the swing. Q3 is the proof.

(d) Sevenum. Unresolved. Management repeats "about 50bp in 2027"; the second set takes €8m, about 20bp, reading 50bp as the later run-rate. I keep the second set's figure, because precedent supports a slow first year (logistics start-ups reach maturity after about 18 months, per Bernstein; Apotea's and Meds' moves both cost margin first). The Consumer should treat the difference, about €11m, as the largest identifiable source of upside to FY27 EBITDA.

(e) Why broker EPS differs. The second set is right that the spread is definitional, and wrong on one detail. Deutsche Bank's FY27 EPS of €1.98 excludes the amortisation of acquired intangibles (about €0.49 a share), the stock option cost (€0.17) and the non-cash part of the convertible's interest (€0.44); those three add-backs, €1.10 in all, are almost the whole distance to Jefferies' reported €0.84. Tax is not the lever between brokers: Deutsche Bank and mwb both use about 25%.

(f) Cash conversion. The second set (free cash flow at 0.4-0.8 times net income) is right against the first set (0.9-1.1 times), because leases and working capital are real cash costs.

Three shared anchors that have moved.

  1. Q3 growth was 17.7%, not the "about 20%" both sets inferred.
  2. The Capital Markets Day is probably not Redcare's (reading notes). The first set's "high novelty" step in the next six months, and the second set's 35% chance of an investment message at that event, both lose their occasion. The chief executive's Q3 statement points the other way, towards profit and cash.
  3. Acquisition. Both sets worried that additions of German Rx customers had slowed to 60-70 thousand a quarter. Q3 added 123,000, after Redcare raised its bonus for new customers. The concern moves from whether patients can be acquired to what they cost.

A consequence of the Rx gross margin correction: the group gross margin percentage will fall faster than either set shows, towards about 20.5% by FY30 instead of 21.0%. This does not touch the forecasts, because the inputs are EBITDA margins and the lower gross margin of Rx is matched by lower costs. The Consumer should simply not quote the second set's gross margin path as firm.

The implication that jars. The first set concluded (C-83) that the balance of upside to downside "becomes attractive only if the Consumer's model sits materially above consensus for FY27-28". The second set's model does not: it is 1% above consensus EBITDA in FY27 and 4% below in FY28. Taken together, the earlier sets therefore say that the case cannot rest on upgrades to EBITDA. It has to rest on delivery itself (the first profitable year and the first positive free cash flow), on the low starting valuation, and on revenue-led guidance raises. I return to this in the fourth set.

Why it is not placed It rates how well the sell-side and investors already articulate the case, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or how much of the case is known.

I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

–46

Layer 4 · Stock market narrative

Judgement: The positive dynamics are fully articulated by the two houses that dominate coverage, and the share price and positioning show that investors accept them only in part. What is thinly articulated is mostly nuance and risk: how the 2026 margin gain was composed, how little the January 2027 fee step adds net of the rebate, the absence of a fee tailwind in H2 2027, the political exposure of the co-payment waiver, and the friction of the PoPP transition. The under-articulated positives are narrower: a cash tax rate below what brokers model, and the fact that the spread of EPS forecasts is a matter of definition. So the narrative already holds most of what is good in our assessment, and less of what is cautious in it.

The evidence base, and its limit. FactSet lists nine brokers. The research in my files is Deutsche Bank's and Jefferies' (current), mwb's model, and Morgan Stanley's older work. Deutsche Bank is the most positive voice ("Profitable growth takes centre stage"; target €104); Jefferies rates the shares a Buy and its top online pharmacy, with a monthly tracker that often carries the cautious data. I found no usable investor commentary from social media. The investor column below rests on price behaviour, on short interest of about 17% (part of it hedging of the convertible), and on valuation. I have asked for more (request OWN-3).

The 5% margin target Deutsche Bank thinks FY28 achievable; Jefferies has 4.7% in FY29; no broker has 5% in FY28 Not visible The scepticism is known We reach 5% around FY30

Three patterns in the table.

  1. Everything that makes the case attractive is known. A reader of the two houses' notes would find the volume engine, the fee, the automation, the cash inflection and the valuation discount set out in full.
  2. What is not articulated is how the pieces interact. No note joins the fee schedule to the rebate and the calendar, the marketing ratio to the gross margin, or the co-payment rise to the politics of the waiver. Our assessment differs from the narrative mainly in these joins, and on most of them it is the more cautious.
  3. Investors are less convinced than analysts. Six of nine brokers say Buy and the median target is €96, yet the shares stood below their level of a year earlier even after the rally, trade at a discount to a smaller and less profitable rival, and carry heavy short interest. The scepticism is about durability: whether a margin gain will be kept, and whether the state will leave the bonus alone. Those are the right doubts, and the next two quarters address the first of them directly.

Secondarily, 18 months to three years. The long-run potential of online prescriptions is well rehearsed. What is not: that the convertible's put ties up cash until 2030; that recognising the tax losses will produce a large one-off gain in reported earnings and then a higher tax rate with no change in cash; and that political pressure rises with online share, so that success brings its own brake. Swiss liberalisation and cross-border prescriptions in the EU are rarely mentioned and are worth little in any case.

How our assessment compares with what the narrative reflects. Against the sell-side, the gap is slightly unfavourable: they say what we would say on the positives, and say less on the cautions. Against investors, it is slightly favourable: we have more confidence than the price implies that the Rx profit stream is durable through 2027 and that FY27 will be the first profitable year. The two roughly cancel.

Layer 1: Assessing the foundational components of an investment case

Written 25 October 2026, 23:49 to 00:10 UK, Opus, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to investigate and research each question, analyse the relevant information and form an insightful judgement regarding it, and to do the same for each group of questions as a whole, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating model and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Redcare Pharmacy N.V. (RDC-DE): First set of questions, the foundational components of the investment case

Written 25 October 2026 by the Investment Analyst (first set), for the Investment Analyst answering the second set (operating and financial model, 18 months to four years).

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Reading notes for the Consumer: the bases, the conflicts in the sources, and the model's spine

These notes fix the definitions used throughout. They matter, because the sources disagree on several load-bearing figures.

  • Price and size. The share price was €77.90 on 23 October 2026. Market capitalisation is €1,603m on 20.6m diluted shares (20.1m basic). Enterprise value is €1,855m on FactSet's definition: debt of €355m, cash of €119m, minorities of €17.8m.
  • Dates that fall inside the model's first quarter. Q3 2026 results are due on 29 October 2026, and the Capital Markets Day (CMD) on 12 November 2026. The CMD is the new chief executive's first.
  • EBITDA basis. All margins here are on the company's adjusted basis, which is also the brokers' basis. History: FY23 €53.5m (3.0%), FY24 €33.3m (1.4%), FY25 €57.4m (2.0%), H1 2026 €44.0m (2.6%), Q2 2026 €29.6m (3.5%, a ten-year high). Q3 2026 was flat on Q2, so about 3.5%. FactSet's "operating" EBITDA is about 26% lower (FY25 €45.6m). Never mix the two bases.
  • Consensus. This is the FactSet median at 25 October 2026. Revenue €3,456m / €3,894m / €4,313m / €4,908m for FY26 to FY29. EBITDA €99m / €138m / €182m / €254m (margins 2.9% / 3.5% / 4.2% / 5.2%). EBIT €21m / €53m / €96m / €183m. Adjusted EPS -€0.21 / €1.32 / €2.85 / €7.23. FY29 rests on one or two brokers only, so treat it as one broker's view, not consensus.
  • Gross margin, two bases. The company's adjusted gross margin was 23.0% in FY24, 22.6% in FY25 and 22.1% in Q2 2026 (23.8% in Q2 2025). FactSet's is lower: 20.2% in FY25 and 20.1% in Q2 2026. The gap is classification. Model on one basis only, and I recommend the company's.
  • German Rx revenue in Q2 2026 was €180m (+58%) per the company's interim report. Jefferies' €205m is a different definition, so do not use it. German Rx was €348m in H1 2026 and grew 56% in Q3 2026. FY26 guidance is €730-760m, or +45% to +51%, which puts FY25 at about €500-505m.
  • Segment shape for FY26, my reconstruction. German Rx is about €745m, or 22% of the group (about 17% in FY25). Swiss Rx through the 51%-owned MediService is about €620m (H1 €308m, +17%). DACH non-Rx is about €1.45-1.5bn (H1 €734m, +9%). International (Belgium, France, Italy, the Netherlands) is about €0.62-0.64bn (H1 about €314m, +17-18%). These sum to the guided €3.41-3.47bn.
  • Leverage, two definitions. FactSet's net debt of €236m includes leases (€77m) and the convertible at book value. On FactSet's operating EBITDA that is 3.6x trailing. The company reports net debt/EBITDA of 1.1x at Q2 2026 (1.3x at year-end 2025) on its own narrower definition. Always name the definition. The only material instrument is the €300m convertible bond: 1.75% coupon, redeemed at 110%, due April 2032. Nothing significant matures before 2030.
  • Sell-side breadth. FactSet shows nine brokers: six Buy or Overweight and three Hold, so a 67% buy share. The "100% Buy" in the Change Forces digest is a Bloomberg August snapshot of the two main brokers, and should not be relied on. Targets run from €71 to €140, with a median of €96.
  • Smaller conflicts. Galenica's stake is 10.4% (August 2026) against an older 8%. Use 10.4%. The CardLink licence runs to 31 March 2028; the January 2027 date in the older sources is superseded.
  • The model's spine, my central judgement. German Rx profit is earned per pack, not per euro of revenue. The regulated pharmacy margin on a German prescription pack is mostly a fixed fee (the Fixum) plus a small percentage of the drug price, less the statutory rebate to insurers. Net of the rebate, that fixed element per pack moves as follows: €8.35 − €1.77 = €6.58 in H1 2026; €9.00 − €1.77 = €7.23 in H2 2026; €9.50 − €2.07 = €7.43 from January 2027, which is +13% on H1 2026. So pack volume and pack frequency drive German Rx gross profit, while drug-price mix (the 14% higher basket from specialty drugs) inflates revenue and dilutes the percentage gross margin without adding much gross profit. The Consumer should model German Rx as packs times gross profit per pack, and the rest of the group on percentage margins.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 2

Research requests for the next set

Company: Redcare Pharmacy N.V. (RDC-DE; formerly Shop Apotheke Europe), Europe's largest online pharmacy, Dutch-domiciled and serving Germany cross-border; segments are DACH (German prescription (Rx) medicines, German and Austrian non-prescription OTC, beauty and supplements, and Swiss Rx through the 51%-owned MediService joint venture with Galenica) and International (Belgium, France, Italy, the Netherlands), plus a third-party marketplace and retail media business

Request: T-P1

Peers: DocMorris AG: the closest listed competitor in German online Rx and OTC; consolidated logistics into automated Heerlen; "AI-First" cost programme. Apotea AB: the Swedish online pharmacy with automated Varberg and Morgongåva hubs; the main precedent for margin progression as online pharmacy penetration rose. Galenica AG: Swiss omnichannel pharmacy and wholesale group; Redcare's joint venture partner; warehouse automation at Lausanne-Ecublens. Meds Apotek AB: a smaller Swedish online pharmacy, as a sub-scale contrast. Farmacosmo SpA: an Italian online pharmacy, as a sub-scale contrast. Chewy Inc.: US online pet retailer and pharmacy with recurring auto-ship orders and automated fulfilment; a precedent for operating leverage in recurring online health retail. Ocado Group: a precedent for automated warehouse economics and commissioning risk. Disapo and Sanicare (private German mail-order pharmacies, insolvent in 2026): precedents for fixed-cost traps at sub-scale. Inputs: What is the split of Redcare's operating cost base between fixed and variable costs: warehouse labour, IT, general and administrative costs and brand marketing, against cost of goods, shipping, picking and packing, payment fees, performance marketing and Rx bonus or co-payment cover? How did Redcare's adjusted EBITDA margin move in each of these periods, and why: 2021 (Sevenum warehouse migration, Rx bonus ban; Q2 2021 growth of 7.3% per Morgan Stanley); 2022 (adjusted EBITDA margin -0.3%); 2023 (MediService consolidation); late 2024 (stepped-up marketing); Q4 2025 (bonus campaigns; a 1.6% margin per Jefferies); Q2 2026 (3.5%, with adjusted EBITDA up 63% on revenue up 20%)? What do the company (Q2 2026 slides and call), Deutsche Bank and Jefferies say about the Sevenum automation programme: the €100m budget; commissioning timing (Q1 2027); the 70% lower labour cost per order from 2027; Deutsche Bank's estimate of about 50 basis points of EBITDA and about €10m of cost efficiencies in 2027; the depreciation it will add; what similar warehouse automation projects at Apotea (Varberg, SEK 17.6m of added depreciation in Q2 2026), Galenica and Ocado actually delivered against plan, and how long they took? What are the wage and cost-per-parcel economics of the Pilsen (Blatnice) site against Sevenum, given that Czech law prevents Rx shipments across the border? Marketing fell to 5.1% of revenue in Q2 2026, down 210 basis points (Deutsche Bank, 29 July 2026), and management says it will stabilise at about 5%. What evidence is there on the floor for marketing intensity in online pharmacy at Redcare, DocMorris (marketing ratio down 310 basis points, H1 2026) and Apotea (a Nordic marketing ratio of about 3% per Carnegie)? Which cost programmes in online pharmacy and e-commerce delivered as promised? Include DocMorris' AI-First programme (at least CHF 15m of annual savings by FY27, about 100 full-time positions, the Ludwigshafen closure) and the Redcare entity mergers of January 2026. Which cost lines proved cuttable, and which did not? How does the 2021 Sevenum migration precedent (sales outlook cut; Q3 2021 sales about 9% below consensus per Morgan Stanley) bear on the risk of the Q1 2027 commissioning, given warehouse staff turnover of 39.1% in 2025 (Annual Report 2025)? Focus: How much of each additional euro of German Rx and OTC gross profit reaches EBITDA and EBIT once Sevenum is commissioned and marketing settles near 5% of revenue, which cost lines rise with volume, and how large a margin swing a revenue shortfall or overshoot of a given size would cause.

Request: T-P2

Peers: DocMorris AG: the direct competitor for German Rx patients through bonuses and co-payment cover; also an OTC rival. Apotea AB: Swedish online price leader that held gross margin while Rx mix rose. Galenica AG: Swiss omnichannel group facing Federal Office of Public Health price cuts. dm-drogerie markt and Rossmann (private): German drugstore chains entering online OTC, and in Rossmann's case planning Rx. gesund.de (wholesaler-backed platform for local pharmacies, with PAYBACK). Amazon: a potential OTC price setter. CVS Group plc: its online pharmacy Animed Direct as a precedent for low-margin online medicine retail under price-comparison pressure. Operating conditions: the German fixed-price Rx regime as the pharmacy fixed fee rises (€8.35 to €9.00 on 1 July 2026; €9.50 on 1 January 2027) while the statutory rebate to insurers rises (€1.77 to €2.07 on 1 January 2027); the 2027 rise in patient co-payments (minimum €5 to €7.50, maximum €10 to €15) and the resulting competition in Rx bonuses and co-payment cover between cross-border mail-order pharmacies; periods of soft OTC and beauty demand and heavy promotion (late 2024, Q4 2025, the July 2026 heatwave); entry of drugstore chains into online OTC (dm since December 2024; Rossmann planned for late 2026); wage and parcel-carrier cost inflation; Swiss regulated price cuts and generic substitution affecting MediService; the pricing of retail media and marketplace commissions to suppliers. Inputs: How much gross profit per German Rx pack do online pharmacies earn after the fixed fee, the percentage margin, the statutory rebate, bonuses or co-payment cover and the €0.19 signature-on-delivery cost (Deutsche Bank, 3 June 2026)? How does this compare between Redcare and DocMorris? How much will Redcare and DocMorris spend covering the 2027 co-payment increase? DocMorris management declined to commit to full cover at its August 2026 roadshow. How do the Federal Court of Justice ruling of 17 July 2025 and the Karlsruhe Higher Regional Court ruling of 9 December 2025 on €25 vouchers constrain the form of bonuses? Redcare's AI pricing tools added about 2 percentage points to German non-Rx growth (Q2 2026 call). Redcare prices up to 10% below competitors on key OTC items (expert calls). Management states it will not raise OTC prices if that compromises customer trust. What evidence is there of OTC price increases achieved and held, or given back? The German non-Rx gross margin rose by over two percentage points quarter on quarter in Q2 2026, and the group gross margin was 22.1% in Q2 2026 against 23.8% a year earlier (company). How much is mix and how much is price? How did OTC pricing behave when online discounters gained share in Sweden? Inderes (23 June 2026) reports store chains cutting beauty prices. How did Apotea hold a 27.5% gross margin in Q2 2026? What do suppliers pay for retail media at Redcare (over 150 pharmaceutical suppliers) and DocMorris (mid-double-digit EBITDA margins per Deutsche Bank)? How durable is that pricing? What are the wholesale purchase terms, statutory surcharge caps and the "ongoing discussions with major pharmaceutical wholesale partners regarding changing purchase conditions" that Redcare management acknowledged? Focus: Whether Redcare can hold or raise gross profit per order and per Rx pack through 2027-29, as Rx mix rises and co-payments rise, without a bonus or price war with DocMorris or the drugstore chains eroding the fixed-fee gains.

Request: T-P3

Why requested: Redcare's margin path depends on whether reinforcing loops (cohort layering, fixed-cost absorption, traffic monetisation, local pharmacy closures) outrun balancing loops (Rx mix dilution, political backlash as online share rises, a recapitalised competitor's response, and working capital absorption). Peers: DocMorris AG: closest competitor; the same loops at about half the scale; TeleClinic telemedicine funnel. Apotea AB: Swedish precedent of online pharmacy share compounding to above 27% of the market. Galenica AG: omnichannel incumbent and joint venture partner, showing the incumbent response. Chewy Inc.: recurring auto-ship cohort economics. Zur Rose Group (now DocMorris): the precedent of the loop running in reverse (cash burn, capital raises). Inputs: Cohort loop. 2025 German Rx cohorts generate about four times the revenue and twice the accumulated gross profit of non-Rx cohorts after six quarters (Redcare, Q2 2026 call and slides). Repeat orders are 90% of orders (Q2 2026 interim report). The mixed-order rate was 40.8% in late 2025. DocMorris reports e-Rx cohorts are 4.5 times more loyal than paper-Rx customers (Deutsche Bank, 24 August 2026). How do retention and order frequency evolve for these cohorts over three to five years? Acquisition loop. Active German Rx customers grew from 1.67m in Q1 2026 to 1.73m in Q2 2026. App downloads were down 18% year on year in September 2026 (Jefferies). CardLink awareness was 35% in Deutsche Bank's March 2025 survey. Do six-month repeat prescriptions (from 1 July 2026), PoPP and Health ID (Phase 2 from Q4 2027) and the 2027 co-payment rise re-accelerate patient acquisition? Fixed-cost loop. Does volume through automated hubs lower cost per order enough to fund bonuses that incumbents cannot legally match, and so drive further volume? Monetisation loop. Does traffic growth raise retail media and marketplace revenue, which then funds price and bonuses? Local pharmacy closure loop. German pharmacies fell below 16,600 in 2026 (Jefferies). Do closures send patients online? Political balancing loop. The Deutscher Apothekertag ban motions (September 2026). Health Minister Linnemann's criticism of bonuses. The Health Finance Commission's €42.3bn 2027 savings agenda. How does political pressure scale with online share? Competitive balancing loop. DocMorris' CHF 200m capital raise and Q3 2026 break-even, with German Rx growth of 78%. Working capital loop. Rx receivables from insurers take over a month to settle, against supplier payables of €252m (H1 2026). Focus: Which of these loops is strongest over 2027-29 and what would stall or reverse it, in particular whether political and competitive balancing forces cap Redcare's margin before the reinforcing cohort and fixed-cost loops lift it to its 5% mid-term target.

Request: T-P4

Why requested: Redcare's EPS is crossing from loss to profit, so small differences in depreciation, finance cost, tax, minorities and share count create very large EPS differences. Brokers' FY27 EPS forecasts range from €0.84 (Jefferies) to €2.04 (Deutsche Bank) on almost identical EBITDA (FactSet, October 2026). Peers: DocMorris AG: similar convertible-funded capital structure and below-EBITDA profile. Apotea AB: mature profitable online pharmacy with a clean EPS bridge. Galenica AG: Redcare's 49% minority partner in MediService. CVS Group plc: a precedent for adjusted-to-reported gaps from tax credits and acquisitions. Inputs: Depreciation and amortisation after Sevenum is commissioned in Q1 2027, including amortisation of capitalised development (restricted legal reserves under Dutch law) and right-of-use assets (€70.8m at end-2025). Net finance cost. Include: the IFRS effective interest on the €300m 1.75% convertible due April 2032 with 110% premium redemption; lease interest on €77.4m of lease liabilities; the €13m Sevenum-secured bank loan; interest income on cash; the effect of the 2026 repurchases and redemptions of the 2021/2028 convertible (Deutsche Bank notes elevated 2026 finance expense). Effective tax rate. 41.2% in H1 2026 on deferred tax from convertible repurchases (interim report). Also: the size and usability of tax losses carried forward, and the Dutch statutory rate. Minority interests. Galenica owns 49% of MediService; MediService dividends of €5.1m in 2025. What is MediService's net profit? Share count and dilution. The convertible's conversion price and the dilution at various share prices; the employee stock option plan (adjusted EBITDA excludes its cost, €1.4m in H1 2026); the 130,000-share managing board option cap. German Rx pack volumes and average pack value. Deutsche Bank's scenarios of 17.6m packs at €50 and 12.5m at €70 for 2027 (3 June 2026). How do the fixed fee and statutory rebate changes flow per pack? Working capital per euro of incremental Rx revenue, and the capex path. Management guides capex below 2% of revenue from FY27. Below-EBITDA assumptions used by Deutsche Bank, Jefferies, Metzler, MP Capital Markets, mwb research, Oddo BHF, Barclays and UBS. Focus: What a realistic bridge from EBITDA to adjusted and reported EPS and free cash flow looks like for FY26-FY29, and which single below-EBITDA assumption (finance cost, tax shelter, minorities or convertible dilution) most explains the spread of broker EPS forecasts.

Layer 2: Translating the foundational components into an operating and financial model for the next 18 months, and 18 months to 3 years out

Written 27 October 2026, 14:17 to 14:36 UK, Opus, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years, based on the first set's answers, a base of the company's historical financial and operating performance, and how businesses in the sector and industry outperform or underperform, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a trustworthy judgement regarding the predictability of delivery of the financial forecasts over the next 18 months.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the likely effect on the company's financial forecasts is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Redcare Pharmacy N.V. (RDC-DE): Second set of questions, the operating and financial model

Written 26 October 2026 by the Investment Analyst (second set), for the Investment Analyst answering the third and fourth sets.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Reading notes for the Consumer: the bases, the base case on one page, and where I depart from the first set

The bases used throughout (do not mix them)

  • EBITDA is the company's adjusted figure, which is also the brokers' basis. It excludes the cost of the employee stock option plan (about €3m a year; €1.4m in H1 2026) and one-off project costs. FY25 was €57.4m (2.0%). FactSet's "operating" EBITDA is about 26% lower (FY25 €45.6m) and is never used here.
  • Depreciation and amortisation (D&A) in the forecast inputs excludes the amortisation of acquired intangibles (the purchase price allocation, "PPA"), as the form requires. Total D&A runs at about €70m a year (H1 2026 €33.9m). The PPA part is not disclosed; I estimate it at about €13m a year (MediService alone explains about €5m: Redcare books a €3.5m consolidated loss for MediService in FY25 against a standalone profit of CHF 0.6m at Galenica; the rest is older customer-base amortisation from Europa Apotheek, medpex, nu3 and smartpatient). This estimate is a known gap and is requested from AlphaSense.
  • Earnings per share comes on two bases in my work. "Model basis" is adjusted EBITDA less D&A excluding PPA, less net finance cost, less tax, less minorities. "Reported-like" additionally deducts PPA and the stock option cost; it is the basis most brokers in FactSet's consensus actually submit (Jefferies and mwb publish reported EPS). FactSet's "consensus EPS" mixes the two bases (P-08).
  • Working capital is FactSet's definition (receivables plus inventories less trade payables): €90.5m or 3.1% of trailing revenue at end-FY25 and €114m or 3.5% at June 2026. The company's own figure (€85.5m, 2.66%, June 2026) uses a wider definition and is not comparable.
  • Q3 2026 is inferred from the 28 September ad hoc release: German Rx +56%, adjusted EBITDA margin flat on Q2 (so about 3.5%), and a segment build that gives group revenue growth of about 20% (about €863m). Results are due on 29 October 2026.

In one sentence: revenue modestly above consensus (+1-2% a year), EBITDA in line in FY27 and 3-15% below consensus from FY28 because I put the company's 5% margin target in FY30 rather than FY28, EPS above consensus mainly for definitional reasons, and free cash flow in line to below consensus.

Where I depart from the first set, and why

  1. The 2026 margin step was mostly a one-off reallocation of acquisition spending, not repeatable fixed-cost leverage. In Q2 2026 the margin rose 100bp (2.5% to 3.5%). Marketing fell 210bp, but the gross margin fell 170bp, of which only about 50bp is explained by Rx mix; the rest is consistent with Rx bonuses, which the company books against gross margin. So the total cost of acquiring patients fell by about 90bp, not 210bp; Rx mix took about 50bp; and genuine leverage on all other operating costs added only about 60bp at 20% growth (P-01). The first set's "8-10% incremental margin" and "5% by FY28 on mechanical levers" both lean on the marketing cut, which cannot recur at the same size.
  2. Q2 and Q3 at 3.5% are peak quarters, not a run-rate. Q1 and Q4 are structurally weaker (Q4 2024 -0.7%, Q4 2025 1.6%, Q1 2025 1.3%, Q1 2026 1.7% by my arithmetic). Adding 80-100bp of levers to 3.5%, as the first set did in C-13, double-counts seasonality; the right annual base is FY26 at about 2.9%, with an H2 2026 exit of about 3.25%.
  3. Consensus revenue is less inconsistent with fast German Rx growth than the first set said. With non-Rx growth of 7.5-8% and Swiss Rx at 10%, consensus group growth of 12.7% for FY27 already implies German Rx growth of about 25%. The first set's "consensus implies 15-20%" rests on its own higher non-Rx assumption. The real swing factor for FY27 revenue is the non-Rx business, not the German Rx narrative.
  4. Sevenum's €10m of 2027 savings is about 25bp of group revenue, not 50bp. Deutsche Bank's "about 50bp" is better read as the run-rate by 2028-29 (about €18-22m), and depreciation of about €9-10m a year absorbs about half of it below EBITDA.
  5. The minority leak through the profit and loss account is close to zero, not €3-5m. MediService earns almost nothing after PPA (standalone EBIT CHF 0.5m on CHF 529m of revenue). The real leak is cash: about €5m a year of dividends to Galenica.
  6. The convertible's terms are now known: conversion price €173.74, a maximum of 1.727m new shares (8.4% of the share count), an IFRS effective interest rate of 5.16% against a 1.75% cash coupon, and (per the AlphaSense report) an investor put in 2030, inside the forecast horizon.
  7. The width of broker EPS (€0.84 to €2.04 for FY27) is mostly a matter of definitions, not operations: PPA, non-cash interest and the tax rate each explain €0.35-0.55 per share.
  8. Tax is more nuanced than "loss carryforwards shelter profits". Dutch law lets losses offset only 50% of taxable profit above €1m, so cash tax runs at about 13-18% of profit even with €312m of unrecognised Dutch losses; and once the company recognises those losses as a deferred tax asset (I assume at end-FY28), the accounting tax rate jumps to about 26% while cash tax stays low.

Part 4. The reasoning behind the inputs

Segments: revenue and margin by segment, where the company reports them

Redcare reports two segments, DACH and International, with adjusted EBITDA for each; within DACH it discloses German Rx revenue (and guides it) and non-Rx growth, and Swiss Rx (MediService, 51% owned) is visible from half-year figures. The split below is my reconstruction on those disclosures (FY25 German Rx from the FY26 guidance of €730-760m being +45% to +51%).

Segment margins (adjusted EBITDA, my estimates): DACH about 2.5% in FY25 (2.6% in FY24; Q2 2026 about €28m, about 4.0%) rising to about 3.4% (FY26), 4.1% (FY27), 4.5% (FY28) and 5.4% (FY30); within DACH, MediService earns very little (standalone EBIT CHF 0.5m on CHF 529m in 2025), so German Rx and non-Rx carry DACH's margin. International moved from -6.2% (FY23) and -3.8% (FY24) to 0.9% in Q2 2026 (€1.4m); I assume about 0.5% for FY26, 1.0% (FY27), 1.5% (FY28), 2.0% (FY29) and 2.5% (FY30), with France the laggard. Group margin: 2.9%, 3.55%, 4.0%, 4.45%, 4.85%.

The operating drivers behind the inputs

  1. German Rx volume (the main driver). Revenue grows 50% (FY26), 27%, 21%, 18% and 16%: active German Rx customers rise about 15-18% a year from 1.73m (Q2 2026) and revenue per customer about 8-10% a year (six-month repeat prescriptions, specialty basket, Fixum). About 16m packs in FY27 at about €60 of revenue per pack. Redcare's share of the German statutory Rx market rises from about 1.4% (FY26) to about 2.45% (FY30). No S-curve acceleration from PoPP is assumed; the CardLink sunset (31 March 2028) is assumed to pass without disruption.
  2. Non-Rx growth: DACH non-Rx +9% (FY26) slowing to +6% (FY30), International +16% slowing to +9%; Swiss Rx +14% (FY26) then +8-10% net of Federal Office of Public Health price cuts.
  3. Gross margin (company basis): 22.6% (FY25), 21.7%, 21.5%, 21.3%, 21.15%, 21.0%. German Rx mix takes 20-30bp a year off the percentage; this is almost neutral at EBITDA because Rx carries about half the fulfilment cost per euro of revenue. The fee step (€9.50 from January 2027, rebate €2.07) adds about €8.5m year on year in FY27; co-payment cover costs about €6m (FY27) rising to €12m (FY29) incremental to 2026.
  4. Marketing: 5.3% (FY26; H1 5.6%, H2 about 5.0%), 5.0%, 4.8%, 4.7%, 4.6%. Track marketing plus Rx bonuses together: in 2026 part of the marketing saving reappeared as bonuses in gross margin.
  5. All other operating costs: 13.5% (FY26) to 11.55% (FY30): fixed-cost absorption about 25-35bp a year, Sevenum savings of €8m (FY27), €18m (FY28), €22m (FY29), €24m (FY30), less wage inflation and new-management investment.
  6. Seasonality (Part 3): Q4 is the promotional quarter and Q1 2027 carries Sevenum commissioning and the start of the co-payment contest, so margins run about 2.8-3.4% in those quarters against about 4.0% in Q2-Q3 2027.
  7. D&A (excluding PPA): about €57m (FY26) rising by about €9m in FY27 as Sevenum (about €100m, five to ten year lives) is depreciated, then growing with capex. Total D&A including PPA is about €70m (FY26), €79m, €82m, €86m and €90m.
  8. Net financial items: the €300m 2032 convertible accretes at its 5.16% IFRS effective rate (about €14.4-15.7m a year, of which about €9-10m is non-cash), lease interest about €3m, other about €1m, less interest income rising with cash. FY26 includes the costs of redeeming the 2021/2028 convertible.
  9. Tax: P&L rate 22% (FY26), 18% (FY27-FY28) while the €312m of Dutch losses are unrecognised and offset only 50% of profit above €1m; 26% from FY29 after I assume the deferred tax asset is recognised at end-FY28 (a one-off credit of about €60-70m in reported earnings, excluded here). Cash tax stays about 15-18% throughout.
  10. Share count: the FY26 change of +0.5m aligns FactSet's 20.1m (end-2025) with the 20.58m shares in issue at 30 June 2026 and FactSet's 20.6m diluted count; then +0.1m a year from option exercises. The convertible (1.727m shares at €173.74) is not converted in the base case.
  11. Capex about €86m in FY26 (Sevenum completion), then about 1.8% of revenue, within the guided "below 2%". Working capital (FactSet basis: receivables plus inventories less trade payables) rises from 3.1% to 3.6% of revenue as Rx receivables from insurers grow. Lease payments are the principal element only (about €8-10m); lease interest (about €3m) is in net financial items. No acquisitions, no buybacks and no dividends (policy, Dutch restricted reserves and the reported 2030 investor put on the convertible). Minorities (Galenica's 49% of MediService) are about €0.5-2m a year in the P&L the cash leak is about €5m a year of dividends, which the model has no line for.
