The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

  1. EBITDA. My inputs are on the company's adjusted basis, which is also the brokers' basis (FY25 €57.4m). FactSet's history line is "operating" EBITDA (FY25 €45.6m); the difference (+26% in FY25) is the stock option cost (about €3m a year), one-off project costs and other adjustments. Do not compute growth from the FactSet history line to consensus.
  2. D&A and EBIT. The form's D&A excludes amortisation of acquired intangibles (PPA). I estimate PPA at about €13m (FY26-FY27), €12m, €11m and €10m; it is not disclosed. Consensus D&A (€70m, €77m, €78m, €84m) is total D&A, and consensus EBIT (€21m, €53m, €96m, €183m) mostly deducts PPA and sometimes the stock option cost. My EBIT excluding PPA is €44m, €74m, €106m, €141m and €178m; my reported-like EBIT (after PPA and stock options) is about €28m, €58m, €90m, €126m and €164m.
  3. EPS. FactSet's consensus EPS (median -€0.21, €1.32, €2.85; FY29 €7.23 from one broker) blends reported EPS (Jefferies, mwb) with adjusted EPS (Deutsche Bank appears adjusted with a low tax charge). My cross-check EPS below is on the model's basis (excluding PPA and stock options, after minorities, on 20.6-21.0m shares). On a reported-like basis (deducting PPA and stock options) my EPS is €0.22, €1.57, €2.85, €3.82 and €5.22, which is the figure to compare with the FactSet median. A one-off deferred tax credit of about €60-70m (about €3 a share), likely at end-FY28, would sit in reported EPS only.
  4. Gross margin. The company's adjusted gross margin (22.6% FY25) is about 2.4 points above FactSet's (20.2%) because of classification; my gross margin path is on the company basis.
  5. Net debt. The company's net debt/EBITDA (1.1x at Q2 2026) excludes leases and uses adjusted EBITDA; FactSet's (3.6x trailing, €236m) includes leases (€77m) and the convertible at book, on operating EBITDA. My net debt figures (P-02) are FactSet-style, on adjusted EBITDA: about 2.4x at end-FY26, falling to 0.3x by FY30.
  6. Free cash flow. My cross-check is after capex (including intangibles), lease principal and interest, cash interest, cash tax and working capital, before dividends to minorities (about €5m a year). FactSet's consensus free cash flow (-€35m, €28m, €64m, €151m) appears to be before lease payments; on that basis my figures are about -€14m, €30m, €57m, €85m and €108m.
  7. Why the model's free cash flow will sit below my cross-check. If the model treats net financial items as cash and applies the P&L tax rate to cash tax, it will understate free cash flow by the non-cash convertible accretion (about €9-10m a year from FY27) and, from FY29, by the gap between the 26% P&L tax rate and the 15-18% cash tax rate (about €13-14m a year). On my inputs the model's free cash flow should be roughly -€37m, €10m, €36m, €51m and €75m; the differences from my cross-check are these two accounting items, not a disagreement about operations.
  8. Minorities. The model has no minorities line; my cross-check EPS deducts €0.5-2m a year (€0.02-0.10 a share).

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: Redcare Pharmacy N.V. (RDC-DE; formerly Shop Apotheke Europe), Europe's largest online pharmacy, Dutch-domiciled and serving Germany cross-border from Venlo/Sevenum; segments are DACH (German prescription (Rx) medicines, German and Austrian non-prescription OTC, beauty and supplements, and Swiss Rx through the 51%-owned MediService joint venture with Galenica) and International (Belgium, France, Italy, the Netherlands), plus a third-party marketplace and retail media business.

Request: T-D1

Peers: DocMorris AG (the closest listed competitor in German online Rx and OTC; recapitalised with CHF 200m, adjusted EBITDA break-even in Q3 2026); Apotea AB (Swedish online pharmacy; the precedent for margin progression through automation as online share rose); Galenica AG (Swiss omnichannel pharmacy group and Redcare's MediService partner); Meds Apotek AB (sub-scale Swedish online pharmacy, as a contrast); Zur Rose Group (the earlier form of DocMorris; the precedent of growth bought with losses ending in disposals and capital raises); Chewy Inc. (recurring online health retail with automated repeat ordering and automated fulfilment); Zalando SE (a European e-commerce leader that moved from growth to margin focus); dm-drogerie markt and Rossmann (private German drugstore chains entering online OTC). Operating conditions: the German fixed-price Rx regime with the pharmacy fee rising to €9.00 per pack on 1 July 2026 and €9.50 on 1 January 2027 while the statutory rebate to insurers rises from €1.77 to €2.07 on 1 January 2027; the 50% rise in patient co-payments in 2027 and the resulting contest in Rx bonuses and co-payment cover; the CardLink licence to 31 March 2028 and the move to Proof of Patient Presence (PoPP) with Health ID from Q4 2027; soft European consumer demand for OTC and beauty; commissioning of warehouse automation in the peak flu season; a change of chief executive (Peter Schmid von Linstow from 1 October 2026); a risk-off equity market. Inputs: Can Redcare sustain German Rx revenue growth after +55%, +58% and +56% in Q1-Q3 2026, given that active German Rx customers grew from 1.67m (Q1 2026) to 1.73m (Q2 2026) and app downloads fell 18% year on year in September 2026 (Jefferies, 8 October 2026)? How plausible is the mid-term adjusted EBITDA margin target of 5% "within three years" (set 4 March 2026, cut from 8%), given adjusted EBITDA margins of -0.3% (FY22), 3.0% (FY23), 1.4% (FY24), 2.0% (FY25) and 3.5% (Q2 2026)? What drove the 2026 improvement (marketing at 5.1% of revenue in Q2 2026, down 210bp; group gross margin 22.1% against 23.8% a year earlier) and how much of it can recur? Will the Sevenum automation (€100m budget, commissioning in Q1 2027, 70% lower labour cost per order) be delivered without the disruption of the 2021 Sevenum migration (Q3 2021 sales about 9% below consensus, Morgan Stanley), given warehouse staff turnover of 39.1% in 2025? Is free cash flow break-even in FY27 credible with capex guided below 2% of revenue? What does the company's guidance record show (FY26 revenue guidance raised on 15 June and 28 September 2026; FY25 growth of 24.1% against a target above 25%; outlook cuts in 2018 and 2021)? Which strengths are demonstrated (for example scale against DocMorris, 90% repeat orders, Rx cohorts worth 3.4-4 times the revenue of non-Rx cohorts per the company, the legal right of EU mail-order pharmacies to pay Rx bonuses confirmed by the Federal Court of Justice on 17 July 2025), and would any have to be acquired, for example retail media at scale, a telemedicine funnel comparable to DocMorris' TeleClinic, or pharmaceutical and regulatory expertise at the top after the leadership change? Focus: Whether Redcare can deliver a steadily rising margin through 2027-28 on strengths it already has (repeat-ordering Rx cohorts, scale, automation) rather than on capabilities it has not yet shown, and how long comparable online pharmacies and e-commerce companies took to move from about 3% to about 5% EBITDA margins, and what separated those that made it from those that did not.

Request: T-D2

Peers: DocMorris AG (same channel shift at about half the scale; the best read on competitive response); Apotea AB (Swedish precedent: adjusted EBIT margin 5.3% in 2021, 1.0% in 2022, 5.2% in H1 2026 per Carnegie and DNB Carnegie); Galenica AG (Swiss incumbent and MediService partner); Meds Apotek AB (sub-scale contrast); Chewy Inc. (repeat-order economics); Ocado Group (automated warehouse commissioning risk and its dispersion of outcomes); Zalando SE (European e-commerce margin outcomes against plans). Operating conditions: the German fee changes of July 2026 and January 2027; the 2027 co-payment rise; the CardLink-to-PoPP transition (CardLink licence ends 31 March 2028); the German Health Finance Commission's proposals for €42.3bn of 2027 savings; weak OTC and beauty demand and seasonal flu and heat effects; automation commissioning; a new chief executive's first Capital Markets Day (12 November 2026). Inputs: How concentrated are the drivers that separate Redcare's next 18 months to four years from FY20-FY25 (German Rx volume; the combined ratio of marketing and Rx bonuses to revenue; Sevenum labour savings; the January 2027 fee step; non-Rx growth; retail media)? How wide has the historical dispersion of margin outcomes been for online pharmacies after a regulatory channel shift (Sweden after e-prescriptions; Germany since the January 2024 e-prescription mandate), and what events widened or narrowed it? How large is Redcare's quarterly seasonality (adjusted EBITDA margin -0.7% in Q4 2024 and 1.6% in Q4 2025, against 3.5% in Q2 2026, per the company and Jefferies) and how does it affect the Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 outcomes? Which below-EBITDA items (amortisation of acquired intangibles, the IFRS effective interest on the 2032 convertible, the tax rate, stock option costs) explain the FY27 broker EPS range of €0.84 (Jefferies) to €2.04 (Deutsche Bank) on adjusted EBITDA of €137-142m (FactSet, October 2026)? How often have forecasts that rested on one or two drivers (automation savings, a fee increase, a marketing reduction) been met at comparable companies, and over what time? Focus: Which one or two drivers decide whether Redcare's adjusted EBITDA and EPS over the next 18 months land near consensus, how wide and how skewed the range of outcomes is, and in particular how much a co-payment cover contest from January 2027 or a Sevenum commissioning problem in Q1 2027 could move it.

Request: T-I1

Peers: DocMorris AG (raised its 2026 guidance twice and reached break-even early; its Q3 2026 results and co-payment stance are the main read-across); Apotea AB (guidance and delivery record during automation ramp-ups); Galenica AG (Q3 2026 trading update; Swiss price cuts); Meds Apotek AB (Nordic online pharmacy margins); CVS Group plc (its online pharmacy Animed Direct as a precedent for low-margin online medicine retail); Medios AG (German specialty pharmaceutical distributor that warned on profit in July 2026 on German reform and pricing). Operating conditions: the German fee increases and rebate change; the 2027 co-payment rise; Sevenum commissioning in Q1 2027; a new chief executive and an Amazon-trained finance director; the end of preliminary quarterly sales releases; risk-off markets and monthly broker trackers of gematik e-prescription and app data. Inputs: FY26 guidance as raised on 28 September 2026: revenue growth 16-18% (about €3.41-3.47bn), German Rx revenue €730-760m (+45% to +51%), non-Rx growth 10-12%, adjusted EBITDA margin 2.5-3.0% unchanged, against Q2 2026 at 3.5% and a Q3 2026 margin described as flat on Q2; what Q4 2026 growth and margin the guidance implies. FactSet consensus at 25 October 2026: revenue €3,456m, €3,894m and €4,313m for FY26-FY28; adjusted EBITDA €99m, €138m and €182m; FY27 EPS consensus up 85% over six months and down 57% over twelve months. The company's record of guiding conservatively under finance director Hendrik Krampe (March 2026 reset, two ad hoc raises), its discontinuation of EBITDA guidance beyond the next year and move to EPS framing, and the gap between consensus FY28 margin (4.2%) and the company's 5% target. What the Q3 2026 results (29 October 2026), the Capital Markets Day (12 November 2026), the January 2027 trading statement and the FY26 results with first FY27 guidance (about March 2027) are likely to contain. How brokers model the cost of co-payment cover from January 2027, Sevenum savings and depreciation, and the new chief executive's possible reinvestment in marketplace, retail media and AI. What DocMorris' Q3 2026 results and guidance imply for Redcare. Focus: Whether Redcare is more likely to raise, meet or cut its margin guidance (not only its revenue guidance) at the events of the next 18 months, and whether consensus FY27-FY28 EBITDA and EPS are more likely to be upgraded or cut once the co-payment contest, Sevenum commissioning and the new chief executive's plan are known.

Request: T-I2

Peers: DocMorris AG (the most-discussed comparison for Redcare; competitive and valuation read-across); Apotea AB (the Nordic template investors cite); Galenica AG (Redcare's partner and a 10.4% shareholder); Zur Rose Group history (the bear template); CVS Group plc (online medicine retail under price comparison). Operating conditions: the German e-prescription channel shift and fee changes; the 2027 co-payment rise; political pressure from pharmacists' associations (ban motions at the Deutscher Apothekertag, September 2026) and Health Minister Carsten Linnemann's criticism of bonuses; the CardLink-to-PoPP transition; a new chief executive; a risk-off, anti-momentum equity market with about 17% short interest in Redcare (partly convertible hedging). Inputs: the composition of Redcare's 2026 margin improvement (the marketing ratio down 210bp to 5.1% in Q2 2026 while the group gross margin fell from 23.8% to 22.1%) and whether Rx bonus costs booked against gross margin offset part of the marketing saving; the seasonal weakness of Q4 and Q1 margins; Sevenum labour savings against the depreciation the project adds; the 2027 co-payment rise as a trigger for competition with DocMorris; the effect of non-Rx growth on consensus revenue; the below-EBITDA definitions behind broker EPS (amortisation of acquired intangibles, the convertible's 5.16% IFRS effective interest against its 1.75% coupon, the Dutch rule that limits loss offset to 50% of taxable profit above €1m, and a possible future recognition of deferred tax assets on €311.9m of unrecognised Dutch tax losses); the 2032 convertible's €173.74 conversion price and any 2030 investor put; political feedback as online share grows; the new chief executive's strategic priorities and their effect on the timing of the 5% margin target. Focus: Which of these dynamics sell-side analysts and investors already discuss and price, and which (in particular the composition of the 2026 margin gain, the below-EBITDA definitions and the convertible's terms) are discussed by only one or two analysts or by no one.

Request: OWN-1

Question: What are the full terms of Redcare Pharmacy N.V.'s €300m 1.75% senior unsecured convertible bonds due 16 April 2032 (issued April 2025): the conversion price (€173.74 at issue) and its adjustment provisions, any investor put dates and put prices (including any put in 2030), issuer call and soft-call conditions, the accreted redemption schedule towards the 110% final redemption, the IFRS split between liability and equity and the 5.16% effective interest rate, the residual €2.6m of the 2021/2028 bonds, and what the company, brokers and convertible investors have said about how the bonds are likely to be settled; and how comparable European convertibles (for example DocMorris' 2028 and 2031 bonds) were handled at their put or call dates?

Request: OWN-2

Question: How large is Redcare Pharmacy N.V.'s amortisation of acquired intangible assets (purchase price allocation) by acquisition (MediService, Europa Apotheek, medpex, nu3, smartpatient and others), with remaining useful lives, how large is its amortisation of capitalised development, and exactly which items each covering broker (Deutsche Bank, Jefferies, mwb research, Metzler, MP Capital Markets, Oddo BHF, Barclays, UBS) excludes from or includes in its adjusted EBIT, adjusted net income and EPS forecasts for FY26-FY28, including their finance cost, tax rate and minority assumptions?

Request: OWN-3

Question: How will Redcare Pharmacy N.V.'s tax charge and cash tax develop as it becomes profitable: the split of its €345.9m of tax loss carryforwards (€311.9m unrecognised Dutch losses at end-2025) by jurisdiction, how the Dutch rule limiting loss offset to €1m plus 50% of remaining taxable profit applies to it, which group profits are earned in Germany (32%) and Switzerland, what the company or auditors have said about when deferred tax assets could be recognised under IAS 12, and what happened to reported EPS and tax rates when comparable loss-making European e-commerce or online pharmacy companies (for example Zalando, HelloFresh, Ocado, DocMorris) first recognised their tax losses?

Request: OWN-4

Question: How does Redcare Pharmacy N.V. account for and spend on German Rx bonuses and co-payment cover (booked against revenue or gross margin, or as marketing), what do they cost per pack and in total, how much of the fall in the group gross margin from 23.8% (Q2 2025) to 22.1% (Q2 2026) came from Rx mix and how much from bonuses, retail media timing or OTC pricing, how does DocMorris account for its equivalent, and what have Redcare, DocMorris, Rossmann and analysts said about co-payment cover after co-payments rise in January 2027, including any evidence from early 2027?

Request: OWN-5

Question: How likely is the German transition from CardLink (licence to 31 March 2028) to Proof of Patient Presence with Health ID (Phase 2 from Q4 2027) to disrupt app-based e-prescription redemptions at Redcare Pharmacy N.V. and DocMorris, both for new and for existing patients: gematik's timetable and readiness, the options to extend CardLink again, Health ID and EU Digital Identity Wallet adoption among patients over 55, what share of Redcare's German Rx redemptions currently run through CardLink, and what precedents (the German e-prescription rollout of 2024, Sweden's BankID-based e-prescription access, other digital identity switches) show about the depth and length of redemption dips?

Request: OWN-6

Question: What has Redcare Pharmacy N.V.'s new chief executive, Peter Schmid von Linstow (in post since 1 October 2026), said or announced about strategy, mid-term targets, investment in marketplace, retail media, AI and telemedicine, cost programmes, disclosure and capital allocation, including at the Q3 2026 results on 29 October 2026 and at the Capital Markets Day on 12 November 2026 if it has taken place, how did brokers and the share price react, and what does his record at AutoScout24, eBay Germany and Visable show about how he balanced investment against margin in his first two years in each role?

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 9 November 2026, 21:08 to 21:32 UK, Fable, ricardinho login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Redcare Pharmacy N.V. (RDC-DE): Third and fourth sets of questions, the predictability of the forecasts and what guidance, consensus and the narrative already reflect

Written 9 November 2026 by the Investment Analyst (third and fourth sets), for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Reading notes for the Consumer: what is new since the earlier sets, the corrections, and the inputs I change

The bases (as in the second set; do not mix them)

  • EBITDA is the company's adjusted figure, which is also the brokers' basis.
  • EPS, model basis, is adjusted EBITDA less depreciation and amortisation excluding the amortisation of acquired intangibles, less net financial items and tax. The 2025 annual report now confirms that amortisation at €12.8m (the second set estimated €13m).
  • EPS, reported-like, also deducts that amortisation and the stock option cost (about €3m). It is the basis to compare with most brokers.
  • The 18 months run from today to May 2028. They contain Q4 2026, all of FY27 and Q1 2028; the co-payment rise and the last fee step (1 January 2027); the Sevenum go-live (Q1 2027); the start of Phase 2 of Proof of Patient Presence (PoPP, Q4 2027); and the end of the CardLink licence (31 March 2028), which therefore falls inside the window, at its end.
  • Price. €77.90 on 23 October 2026 is the latest in my files. The shares fell on the Q3 results of 29 October (Deutsche Bank); I have no later price.

What has happened since the earlier sets were written (25 and 26 October 2026)

1. The Q3 2026 results of 29 October. The earlier sets had only the preliminary release.

Item Actual What the second set assumed
Group revenue €846.7m, +17.7% about €863m, +20.0%
German Rx revenue €197m, +56.3% +56%
Non-Rx growth +9.4%; German non-Rx +9%; October began below +10% (late cough and cold season) about +11% for the year
Adjusted EBITDA €28.7m, 3.4% (2.4% a year earlier); nine months €72.7m, 2.9% 3.5%
Segment margins DACH 4.2%; International −0.4% (+0.9% in Q2; nine months −€2.7m) International about +0.5% for FY26
Gross margin; marketing 21.8% (22.1% a year earlier); 4.9% of revenue (5.9%)
Active German Rx customers 123,000 added in Q3, about double Q2's addition; total about 1.85m to 1.90m (the reports give 1.90m, which does not reconcile with 1.73m plus 123,000) about 1.85m at year-end
Cash €132.4m; +€15m in the quarter, helped by seasonal working capital that reverses in Q4; positive free cash flow in FY27 is now a formal target
Guidance unchanged; it implies Q4 revenue growth of 8-16% and a Q4 margin of 1.4-3.4% (Deutsche Bank)
Sevenum equipment installed and in testing, which "has progressed very well"; go-live Q1 2027; management repeated about 50bp of margin in 2027 (Deutsche Bank, 30 October) €8m, about 20bp, in FY27

The new chief executive's first public statement (Q3 call) was that he will concentrate on execution and monetisation, not on a strategic reset: better e-commerce basics, AI to lower cost, and a marketplace and retail media business that are "in their infancy" and will be scaled slowly. Retail media grew more slowly than revenue in the quarter.

2. The Capital Markets Day on 12 November 2026 is very probably DocMorris', not Redcare's. Both earlier sets treat it as the new Redcare chief executive's first strategy day and build much of their six-month risk around it. The earliest source for the date in the first set's files is a Jefferies note of 15 October 2026, and the Jefferies note of that date in my files is its note on DocMorris' Q3. Jefferies' tracker of 3 November says DocMorris will set out its 2027 co-payment strategy at its Capital Markets Day on 12 November, and the AlphaSense report on the new chief executive says Redcare "has not held" one and that he pointed to the trading update of 11 January 2027. Two other reports still attribute the day to Redcare. I judge the DocMorris reading right, and I have asked for it to be confirmed (request OWN-1). What it changes:

  • No Redcare target-setting event is scheduled before the trading update of 11 January 2027 and the FY26 results with the first FY27 guidance on 3 March 2027.
  • 12 November still matters for Redcare, as the day its only scaled rival states how much of the higher co-payment it will cover.

3. The political risk has become a live initiative. Stephan Pilsinger, a CSU member of the Bundestag, asked the Health Ministry in late October 2026 to prohibit EU mail-order pharmacies from waiving statutory co-payments, through an amendment to § 43c of the Social Code inside the pending digital health act (GeDIG). A second proposal, made at the pharmacists' congress, would have insurers collect co-payments directly, which would remove the waiver mechanically. Both face EU-law and administrative obstacles and neither is government policy. No broker note in my files discusses the Pilsinger initiative; the trade press does.

4. The first broker changes after Q3 were flat to slightly down. Deutsche Bank's FY27 adjusted EBITDA is €134m and its EPS €1.98 (30 October), against €138m and €2.04 in FactSet on 12 October. mwb's FY27 net income is €32m, against about €33m implied before. Jefferies is unchanged (EPS €0.84, target €97, with scenarios of €35 and €120). Jefferies' tracker shows app downloads down 32% year on year in October.

5. Two errors in the AlphaSense reports that the Consumer should not carry into the memo. One report attributes TeleClinic to Redcare; it belongs to DocMorris, and Redcare still has no owned telemedicine funnel. The tax report argues that Redcare's profit will arise in Germany and be taxed at 32%; I disagree, and side with the second set. The pharmacy that sells to German patients is a Dutch company, which is the very fact that gives it the right to pay bonuses, so the profit arises in the Dutch tax group, where the €311.9m of unrecognised losses sit.

The model's cross-check gap, explained

The summary flags one gap: FY2026 EPS of €0.82 in the second set's cross-check against €0.75 in the model. It has three parts, and one of them shows a wrong input.

  1. The share count (a wrong input; about €0.03 of the gap, and about 4% of EPS in every year). Dividing the model's net income by its EPS gives the share count the model uses: about 21.45m in FY26, 21.55m in FY27 and 21.85m in FY30. The second set intended 20.6m rising to 21.0m, and 20.58m shares were in issue at 30 June 2026. The second set's input of +0.5m for FY26 was meant to carry FactSet's end-2025 count of 20.1m to the June 2026 count; the model's figures show that its starting count already includes those shares, so the 0.5m is counted twice. I change the FY26 input to 0.0. The Financial Analyst should confirm that the model's count at the end of FY26 is then about 20.6m to 20.9m; any remainder above 20.6m is not explained by the inputs.
  2. Tax in FY26 (not an input error; about €0.035). The model's FY26 net income of €16.2m is 74.7% of its pre-tax profit, an effective rate of 25.3% against the 22% input. In FY27 to FY30 the model's net income equals pre-tax profit less the input rate exactly. FY26 differs, most probably because the model holds the actual first half, in which tax was distorted by the convertible redemption. I leave the input.
  3. The cross-check itself (about €0.02). The second set's €0.82 did not deduct its own minority charge of €0.5m; on its stated basis it should read €0.80.

The gaps in free cash flow (for example €18m against €9.8m in FY27) are not flagged and are fully explained by the second set's own bridge: the model treats the non-cash part of the convertible's interest (about €9-10m a year) as cash and, from FY29, charges cash tax at the accounting rate. No input is wrong. The Consumer should know that the model's free cash flow of record therefore understates the cash the company will hold by about €8m to €20m a year, which matters for the 2030 put.

I keep every other input, including the growth rates for FY27 to FY30 and every margin. My reasons are in D-01, D-02 and D-04. The effect: revenue of about €3,461m in FY26 and €3,925m in FY27 (0.2% and 0.8% above consensus), adjusted EBITDA of about €100m and €139m (1% above consensus in both years) and about €175m in FY28 (4% below), and EPS of about €2.2 in FY27 on the model basis and about €1.55 on the reported-like basis.

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of RDC-DE with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-06 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Redcare Pharmacy N.V. (RDC-DE; formerly Shop Apotheke Europe), Europe's largest online pharmacy, Dutch-domiciled and serving Germany cross-border from Venlo/Sevenum; segments are DACH (German prescription (Rx) medicines, German and Austrian non-prescription OTC, beauty and supplements, and Swiss Rx through the 51%-owned MediService joint venture with Galenica) and International (Belgium, France, Italy, the Netherlands), plus a third-party marketplace and retail media business.

Request: OWN-1

Question: What has been said and published about Redcare Pharmacy N.V. (RDC-DE) and its closest competitor DocMorris AG since Redcare's Q3 2026 results of 29 October 2026, and in particular at the Capital Markets Day held on 12 November 2026? First establish whose event that was: sources disagree on whether the Capital Markets Day of 12 November 2026 was Redcare's or DocMorris' (Jefferies' "German eRx Data Tracker - October 2026" of 3 November 2026 refers to DocMorris' Capital Markets Day and its 2027 co-payment strategy), and state whether Redcare has held or scheduled a Capital Markets Day of its own. Then report: (1) what DocMorris said about covering German statutory prescription co-payments after they rise by 50% on 1 January 2027 (which patients and orders, at what cost per pack, how it accounts for the cost), about its Rx bonuses, its marketing budget and its financial targets for 2027 and beyond; (2) anything Redcare's chief executive Peter Schmid von Linstow or finance director Hendrik Krampe have said since 29 October 2026 about Redcare's own co-payment cover and Rx bonus from January 2027, fourth-quarter 2026 trading (German Rx, non-Rx, margin), the Sevenum automation go-live in Q1 2027, the mid-term target of a 5% adjusted EBITDA margin "within three years" set on 4 March 2026, a move to guidance on earnings per share or free cash flow, and the bank loan from Deutsche Bank for the Sevenum project mentioned with the Q3 2026 results (amount, amount drawn, interest rate, maturity); (3) how each covering broker (Deutsche Bank, Jefferies, mwb research, Metzler, MP Capital Markets, Oddo BHF, Barclays, UBS and any other) changed its FY2026, FY2027 and FY2028 revenue, adjusted EBITDA and EPS forecasts, its rating and its target price for Redcare after the Q3 2026 results and after 12 November 2026, giving the old and new figure, the date and the definition of EPS each broker uses; and (4) how the share prices of Redcare and DocMorris moved on 29 October 2026 and on and after 12 November 2026, and why commentators said they moved. Date every item, quote the source, and say where no evidence was found.

Request: OWN-2

Question: How likely is it, and on what timetable, that German law or regulation will restrict the Rx bonuses and waivers of statutory co-payments that EU mail-order pharmacies such as Redcare Pharmacy N.V. (RDC-DE) and DocMorris AG offer to German patients, or will reduce the remuneration they earn per prescription pack, and what would each measure do to Redcare? Cover: (1) the initiative of CSU Bundestag member Stephan Pilsinger, reported in the German pharmacy trade press in late October and early November 2026, to prohibit EU mail-order pharmacies from waiving co-payments through an amendment to § 43c SGB V within the draft Act on Data and Digital Innovation in Healthcare (GeDIG): its exact text and status, the positions of the Federal Ministry of Health, Health Minister Carsten Linnemann, the coalition parties and the statutory health insurers, the legislative timetable of the GeDIG, and legal assessments of whether such a prohibition is compatible with EU law and with the Federal Court of Justice ruling of 17 July 2025 on Rx bonuses; (2) the proposal made around the Deutscher Apothekertag that statutory health insurers collect co-payments directly from patients; (3) the German Health Finance Commission's proposals for €42.3bn of savings in 2027 and any resulting bill, in so far as they touch the pharmacy fixed fee (€9.50 per pack from 1 January 2027), the statutory pharmacy rebate to insurers (€2.07 per pack from 1 January 2027), patient co-payments, or the annual review of pharmacy remuneration due from 2028; (4) what Redcare, DocMorris, the European Association of E-Pharmacies, the pharmacists' association ABDA and each covering broker have said about these measures; (5) what share of Redcare's and DocMorris' German Rx orders or customers receive a co-payment waiver or bonus, and its cost per pack, where disclosed or estimated by brokers; and (6) the precedent of the German ban on Rx bonuses for statutory patients under the 2020 On-Site Pharmacy Strengthening Act (Vor-Ort-Apotheken-Stärkungsgesetz), after which Shop Apotheke Europe (now Redcare) lowered its 2021 growth outlook: what happened to the German Rx revenue, customer numbers, guidance and share prices of Shop Apotheke Europe and Zur Rose/DocMorris in the following four to eight quarters. Date every piece of evidence, separate enacted law from proposals, and say where the evidence is thin.

Request: OWN-3

Question: How do sell-side analysts and investors currently value Redcare Pharmacy N.V. (RDC-DE), what do investors as distinct from brokers say about the company, and how did the stock market value comparable companies in the two years in which they first became profitable? Cover: (1) for each covering broker (Deutsche Bank, Jefferies, mwb research, Metzler, MP Capital Markets, Oddo BHF, Barclays, UBS and any other), the valuation method behind its current target price for Redcare (discounted cash flow, enterprise value to sales, enterprise value to EBITDA, price to earnings), the multiple and the forecast year it applies, its discount rate and terminal assumptions, and any scenario values (for example the base, upside and downside values of €97, €120 and €35 in Jefferies' "German eRx Data Tracker - October 2026" of 3 November 2026), with dates; (2) the multiples at which Redcare, DocMorris AG and Apotea AB trade now on forward sales, EBITDA and earnings, and the explanations brokers give for Redcare's discount to DocMorris on enterprise value to sales; (3) precedents: the forward price-to-earnings, enterprise-value-to-EBITDA and enterprise-value-to-sales multiples the market paid for Apotea, Zalando, Chewy, HelloFresh, Ocado, Galenica and DocMorris in the first two years after each first reported positive net income or positive free cash flow, and how those multiples then changed; (4) investor commentary on Redcare that is not broker research: fund manager letters and interviews, short-seller arguments, German and English investor forums and social media (for example X/Twitter and wallstreet-online), with the main bull and bear arguments, how often each appears and since when; (5) the level and recent change of short interest in Redcare (about 17% of shares in autumn 2026 per broker and expert commentary), the named holders of disclosed short positions, and estimates of how much of it is hedging of the €300m 1.75% convertible bonds due 2032; and (6) changes since August 2026 in the holdings of Redcare's main shareholders (Galenica, MK Beleggingsmaatschappij, Smallcap World Fund), directors' dealings by the managing and supervisory boards including the new chief executive, and Redcare's position against the entry thresholds of the MDAX and the STOXX Europe 600. Date every item, credit each view to its own source, and say plainly where nothing was found.
